## [2026-06-09T00:37:37Z] assistant_thought (afterAgentThought)

The document outlines coalition mechanics bridging elections and governance. I am identifying additional angles to consider.

The document already covers leverage versus reprisal, toleration agreements, district archetypes, three reform types, reprisal spikes, anarchy failure, and drone abstentions. I am now looking for angles that remain unexplored or only lightly touched.

Two angles remain unexplored.

First, time and cadence mismatches. Districts rotate monthly, executives sit in December, and guilds rotate quarterly. When do coalitions form relative to these seat changes? Do they collapse instantly or require renegotiation?

Second, guild and executive chambers. The document focuses on district and city executive power. It does not clarify whether guild votes gate reforms too.

Three more angles remain uncovered.

Hate parties are only excluded from pro-democratic coalitions. Their role in anarchy, whether they benefit from collapsed governments, and whether the player can use them tactically are all unexplored.

Indepenents are treated as filler within coalitions. The document does not explain how independent seats affect coalition math across the entire city.

The document also omits how the player earns leverage beyond toleration. It never defines the leverage economy or its sourcing mechanisms.

The document needs an explicit feedback loop: actions drain the leverage budget, which drives coalition maintenance, then reprisals force the player to earn more leverage.

It also lacks a double-entry system for leverage. Corporate and syndicate groups each carry their own leverage pool. It is unclear whether paying toleration debits a specific group account or a shared committed balance.

Corporate wars are another gap. The document does not specify whether at-war corporations still trigger reprisals against each other.

War may limit coalition options. Can the player tolerate only one side during a conflict?

City phases are not fully aligned with the phase system. The free year after a tether snap might need to match the phase unlock schedule.

The monthly player experience is missing. The document does not specify whether the player negotiates coalitions between elections or only during voting. It also omits card actions, district scenes, and the opportunity cost of organizing.

Opposing groups may act independently. Corporations and syndicates could form their own coalitions or outbid the player for toleration.

Anarchy mentions bribing politicians but not proactive corporate coalition-building.

The document needs a win condition. It is unclear whether the goal is perpetual balance-of-power or a phase transition to a democratic majority.

The leverage model must handle the player starting with outright D control. It is unclear how the system behaves when the player holds a majority rather than kingmaker seats.

Economic feedback is missing. Pro-worker reforms shift money and tie to economic assets. Losing a corporation's home district government may not mechanically alter its IG membership or drone votes.

Crime and government control are uncoupled. Anti-crime reforms do not yet link to police or militia effectiveness, despite militia organizing living in districts.

Public opinion and legitimacy are absent. There is no democratic mandate stat beyond IG leverage. Events like academic freedom and conventions do not yet modify the electorate runtime.

## [2026-06-09T00:37:46Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Your doc nails the **core fantasy**: elections produce seat math, but **governing** is a separate game of leverage, toleration, and reform reprisal. The coalition checker as a first step is sensible — it forces you to formalize the rules before building UI.

Here are angles worth working through, grouped by what tends to bite you during implementation.

---

### 1. Two ledgers: leverage you *have* vs leverage you *owe*

You describe spending leverage to buy toleration and to survive reprisal after reforms, but the game already has per-IG `*_leverage` / `*_reprisal` driving attitude labels (`attitude_runtime.scene.dry`). You need to decide:

- Does toleration **debit** `soylent_leverage` directly, or is there a separate “committed / reserved” pool?
- Is coalition maintenance a **monthly tick cost** or only re-checked at elections / on reform?
- When a corp knocks you out of toleration, do you get leverage back or is it sunk?

Without this, “greyed out coalition” vs “possible but unaffordable” will be ambiguous. That’s basically a **resource budget** problem — same idea as upkeep in 4X games.

---

### 2. Calendar: when does control actually change?

Elections are staggered (district months, executive in December, guild seats rotate quarterly in `election_runtime`). Your doc mixes “every time the district votes,” “free year after tether snap,” and “negotiate anytime in the district scene.” Clarify:

| Trigger | What updates? |
|--------|----------------|
| District election month | Seats only, or seats + forced coalition renegotiation? |
| Mid-term reform | Immediate reprisal + maintenance check, or wait until next vote? |
| Monthly tick | Passive upkeep drain on leverage? |
| Cable-break grace year | Auto-control with no leverage cost, or subsidized? |

Hung councils and ties matter too — today `dominant_party` is `0` when no single party leads. Your coalition logic needs explicit rules for **plurality vs majority vs “nobody can govern.”**

---

### 3. The other chambers: guilds and executive

Most of the doc is district + city government. The sim already tracks **Exec** and **Gold/White/Blue guild** seats. Cable negotiations already gesture at “get the votes” for civil rights. Ask:

- Which reforms need **district only**, **city executive**, **guild**, or **all three**?
- Can the player hold Aurora district but lose city executive to a corp flunky — and what can they still do?
- Do guild seats enter coalition math, or stay as background poll pressure?

Otherwise district reforms and city-wide reforms will feel disconnected from what `status.scene.dry` already shows the player.

---

### 4. Where leverage *comes from* (the full loop)

Toleration is one **source** when you’re kingmaker. The organizing phase already moves leverage/reprisal (contracting, cable neg, sabotage under ceasefire, etc.). Spell out the loop:

**Organizing / events → leverage surplus → buy toleration / maintain government → reforms → reprisal + electorate shifts → new election math → repeat or collapse to anarchy**

If earning leverage and spending it live in different card decks with no shared budget UI, players won’t feel that elections “matter” — they’ll feel like two mini-games.

---

### 5. Active opposition, not just passive thresholds

Anarchy describes corps/gangs **bribing politicians** when nobody governs — good. Also consider whether IGs **actively assemble coalitions** without you:

- Can Magrail + Brazos tolerate each other and cut you out?
- Do they bid against you for your swing seats?
- Does a corp at war refuse toleration with the player’s preferred partner?

Your at-war reprisal exception for pro-worker reforms hints at this; extend it to **who will sit in coalition with whom**, not just how much leverage you need.

---

### 6. Hate (H) and Independents (I) as more than exclusions

Pro-democratic reforms define two clean paths that exclude H. Worth deciding:

- **H**: spoiler in hung councils? beneficiary of anarchy? usable for short-term anti-corp votes at a long-term cost?
- **I / Deepers**: are I seats fungible “wildcards,” or does Deeps need a trust track before I seats count toward *any* coalition?
- **Aurora (D majority)**: different rules when you have an outright majority vs when you’re kingmaker elsewhere — holding Aurora may be cheap in leverage but expensive in attention when Pitts/Docks flip volatile.

---

### 7. Reform catalog: scope, sequencing, and “breaking out”

You have three reform **families** with different coalition shapes. Still open:

- **Ordering** — must pro-worker precede pro-democratic (drones freed)? Can judiciary / police improvements gate heavier reforms?
- **Magnitude** — one big “free the drones” vs many small electorate modifiers from `electorate_runtime`?
- **Victory vs treadmill** — is the win condition “democratic majority, less dependent on toleration,” or perpetual balance-of-power? That changes how punishing post-reform reprisal should be.

The pro-democratic “hold until next election” clause is strong — it’s a **delayed payoff** design. Worth specifying what the player sees in projections when drone abstention stacks start moving.

---

### 8. Economic and crime feedback (already in the codebase)

Pro-worker reforms “shift money” — that should hook to `economic_assets_runtime` (prosperity, housing, contracting). Anti-crime hooks to `crime_paramilitary` and cards like `security` / militia organizing. Government control making police/militia “more effective” needs a concrete stat (QNM bump? crime_relief? loyalty?).

Corp **losing home district** should probably feed back into election blocks (drone IG behavior, prosperity) — you mention drones abstaining; tie that to existing electorate modifier patterns so it’s deterministic and visible in projections.

---

### 9. Overlap with cable-break / phase 2

`organize_phase_start` and `cable_break` already establish: corp distraction → city government window → Aurora as base → phase 2 unlocks city affairs. Your “free year after tether snap” should map cleanly onto `phase`, `Exec_*` seats, and existing cable reprisal helpers — otherwise you’ll duplicate executive control logic.

---

### 10. Player-facing clarity

The coalition checker is backend-first; plan the **player questions** it answers:

- “If I pass X reform, which coalitions still hold afterward?”
- “Which district should I give up to afford this vote?”
- “What happens if I do nothing this month?”

Forecasting already exists via `Q.update_projections()`. Showing **coalition feasibility** next to seat projections (before and after a reform) would sell the fantasy better than greyed-out buttons alone.

---

### 11. Failure ladder

Anarchy → gang ascendancy is a good third rail. Also define intermediate states:

- Corp flunky executive but player holds some districts
- Player holds city but loses Aurora
- Partial reform passed then government collapses mid-implementation (repeal? frozen half-done?)

And whether any of this touches `game_over` vs just a harder midgame.

---

### 12. Determinism checklist

Per your project rules, coalition possibility should be a pure function of `Q` + seat state — no hidden rolls. Document:

- Exact coalition templates (which party combos are valid)
- Per-template leverage cost formula (you mention scaling by seat share; specify rounding and multi-IG sums)
- Reprisal spikes from reforms (fixed stacks vs proportional)

That makes the first function you proposed testable via `simulate-election:v2` style extraction later.

---

### Suggested next doc sections

If you extend `political_power.md`, these four headings would close the biggest gaps:

1. **Leverage accounting** (pools, upkeep cadence, toleration grants)
2. **Chamber map** (which body passes what)
3. **Coalition template table** (valid combos, costs, who can veto)
4. **Reform registry** (each reform → coalition requirement, reprisal, electorate delta, maintenance check)

I can help draft any of those against the current election/attitude APIs if you want to go deeper on one angle first — the leverage ledger vs the coalition template table is probably the highest-leverage fork.

## [2026-06-09T00:59:54Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Your additions close several of the gaps from before. The **bandwidth** model in particular is a strong fit for what you already have — `leverage` and `reprisal` stay as the IG relationship state, and coalition maintenance becomes **committed slack** rather than burning leverage. That maps cleanly onto the attitude grid in `attitude_runtime.scene.dry` without inventing a second currency.

Here’s what reads well, what’s still fuzzy, and a few new angles your own text raises.

---

### What’s much clearer now

**Bandwidth vs debit.** “Attitude stays transactional, you just can’t use that slack elsewhere” is the right call. Players can read status tooltips and understand why a coalition option greyed out without feeling punished for governing.

**Calendar triggers.** “Update after district election or when you can’t afford it; district menu can force it sooner” gives you three concrete hooks: election tick, affordability check, player-initiated negotiation. That’s enough to implement without hand-waving monthly upkeep — unless you later want passive drift.

**Grace year as external constraint.** Framing the post–tether-snap year as “corps need you visible for off-world legitimacy, not because you’ve won them over” is good oligarchy flavor. It also explains why reprisal can exceed leverage temporarily without breaking the story beat.

**H as dead weight.** The “refuses minority role, planning seizure” rule is a clean reason H never enters coalitions and why high H poll is dangerous — those seats are **unbuyable** in hung councils, which pushes toward anarchy or forces pro-democratic paths that exclude H.

**Scope fence.** Limiting outcomes to toleration / control / anarchy, and treating “everyone hates us more than each other” as endgame war, is a reasonable v1 boundary. You’re not trying to simulate full parliamentary AI.

**Vote share double effect.** More seats → more leverage from toleration trades *and* cheaper self-government is a nice organizing reward. It ties the election sim to the power loop without requiring outright majorities everywhere.

---

### Tensions worth resolving while the doc is still short

**1. Is bandwidth per-IG or global?**

You describe securing Soylent’s support costing “3 leverage” of surplus while staying transactional with Soylent. Unclear:

- Is each IG’s bandwidth `(leverage − reprisal)` minus commitments **to that IG**?
- Or one **city-wide pool** of surplus you allocate across Soylent + Families + …?

Per-IG is easier to explain in tooltips (“Soylent: 2 bandwidth free, 3 committed to Vats toleration”). Global pool creates harder tradeoffs (“I can’t afford city exec because I’m propping up two district corps”). Both work — pick one early because the coalition checker math depends on it.

**2. Reform reprisal shrinks bandwidth without spending anything.**

Old section: reforms spike reprisal; you must still afford government after. New section: commitments don’t lower leverage, they consume bandwidth above reprisal.

Those compose naturally **if** reprisal increases reduce available bandwidth automatically. Worth one explicit sentence: *“A reform that adds reprisal can collapse control even when no new toleration was bought — your surplus shrank.”* That’s probably the main skill expression in the system.

**3. Grace year: what exactly happens at expiry?**

You say forced renegotiation when bandwidth runs out, and again after the year. Still open:

- Silent flip to corp flunky if no deal?
- Blocking event / card forcing negotiation?
- Does grace **pause** bandwidth checks, or only prevent **removal** while checks still run?

Worth nailing so implementation doesn’t fork “grace” into two different behaviors.

**4. “Voluntarily negotiate for control” — when would a player want that?**

A few natural triggers you might document:

- **Downgrade** — shed expensive district control to free bandwidth for a reform vote elsewhere.
- **Swap** — drop Soylent toleration in Vats to pick up Families toleration in Pitts after an election shift.
- **Preempt** — renegotiate before an expected reprisal spike so you’re not surprised at election time.

Any of those could be a district-scene choice without new mechanics.

**5. Corp flunky without player coalition.**

You scope out corp-led coalitions, but line 13 still has “corporate flunky put back in charge” when the player can’t buy a majority. That’s a fourth outcome at **city** level unless flunky = “whoever had Exec seats before” with no negotiation sim. One line clarifying *“corp flunky is default seat math, not a negotiated corp coalition”* would keep scope honest.

---

### New angles your additions suggest

**Bandwidth UI on status.** Since attitude already shows leverage/reprisal, committed bandwidth could appear as a third number or a subtitle: “3 committed (Vats toleration).” Players otherwise won’t see why they’re “transactional” but still blocked.

**Toleration geography.** You imply tolerating a corp in *their* home district when they’ve lost majority. Can the player also tolerate Soylent in **non-home** districts (e.g. to pass city exec)? If yes, that’s a different leverage grant size or different bandwidth cost — home-district loss is “a big blow” per line 11.

**H growth as anarchy accelerator.** With H unbuyable, refugee / hate stacks (`refugee_pressure_hate` in electorate) become a **governance tax** — not just poll damage, but fewer workable hung-council permutations. That’s worth calling out as intentional.

**Endgame war trigger.** “More afraid of the player than each other” is good narrative escape from toleration sim, but you’ll eventually want a deterministic condition — e.g. multiple IGs at `hostile`/`furious` while inter-corp war flags are off, or combined reprisal above a threshold. Doesn’t need numbers now; just flag it as “define before shipping phase 3.”

**Aurora deferral is fine, but one invariant helps.** Even if loss-of-Aurora is later, v1 might assume **D seats in Aurora don’t require toleration bandwidth** (outright majority) while **using Aurora’s organizational capital to fund bandwidth elsewhere** is the intended loop. Stops Aurora from accidentally using the same machinery as Vats on day one.

---

### Still untouched (only if you care before coding)

- **Guild / executive split** — grace year is city; district control is per-district. Guild seats still aren’t in the doc; fine if reforms are district-scoped first.
- **Coalition template table** — still the best next artifact after the checker function: valid party combos, which IGs must be paid, minimum seat thresholds.
- **Deterministic cost formula** — “scales by seat share” + “3 leverage when transactional” wants one worked example (e.g. hung Vats council, 4 C / 3 D / 2 I, player holds 3 D + 2 I…) so the checker has a test case.

---

Overall the doc went from “good concept” to “implementable v1 spec” with the bandwidth and calendar paragraphs. The highest-leverage next sentence is probably whether bandwidth is **per-IG or pooled**, and whether **reprisal spikes automatically trigger affordability checks** outside of election months.

Want me to draft a small coalition-template table or a worked bandwidth example for one district hung council?

Enough information has been gathered to build the coalition template table.

District councils have 15 seats each. The executive has 150 seats. Parties include C, G, D, H, and I. IGs split into corporate and syndicate groups. Home districts map to specific zones. Aurora starts with D control.

Three governance modes are defined: player toleration offers, direct control purchases via bandwidth, and anarchy. The coalition table will map who can govern, which IGs require bandwidth payments, and the direction of toleration. Player leverage only applies when they tolerate corporations rather than receiving it from them.

District thresholds are 8 seats; executive thresholds are 76. Five coalition states are mapped:

- D outright majority
- D plus I majority
- Hung council with player kingmaker
- Player toleration offer to corporations
- Player-bought coalition via bandwidth

Two additional coalition states are added. Corporate self-majority is the default state with no player negotiation. Anarchy occurs when no eight-seat coalition exists even with player toleration offered to corporations.

Pro-democratic reform paths are defined. One path requires gangs and independents without corporations or hate groups. The other path requires non-home corporations and independents without gangs or hate groups.

The document states that pro-democratic reform requires majority support under both coalition structures. This creates ambiguity: it may require both paths to exist simultaneously, or it may allow the reform to pass under either structure.

The per-group bandwidth rule, reprisal-as-shrink, grace-year expiry, and voluntary renegotiation as swap/toleration all fit together cleanly now. The doc is in good shape to hang a table on.

Below is a **v1 coalition template table** aligned with your sim: **15 seats per district** (majority = **8**), **150 executive seats** (majority = **76**), parties **C/G/D/H/I**, IGs **Soylent / Magrail / Brazos / Families / Edges / Sharks**.

---

### IG ↔ party ↔ home district (reference)

| IG | Kind | Votes as | Primary home district | Notes |
|----|------|----------|----------------------|--------|
| Soylent | corp | C | **Vats** | Drone-heavy; toleration target when C loses Vats |
| Magrail | corp | C | **Railyard** | Also strong in Docks |
| Brazos | corp | C | **Limelight** | |
| Families | syndicate | G | **Pitts** | Bandwidth is per-IG — can't pay Sharks for Families support |
| Edges | syndicate | G | Aurora (presence) | |
| Sharks | syndicate | G | Pitts / Limelight | |
| — | — | **D** | Aurora (player base) | Outright majority → control without IG bandwidth |
| — | — | **I** | Deeps, Docks, Aurora | Wildcard; Deepers trust track later |
| — | — | **H** | — | **Never** in player coalition; seats are dead for deals |

---

### Table 1 — District control outcomes

| ID | Seat condition | Who governs | Player role | IG bandwidth | Toleration / leverage |
|----|----------------|-------------|-------------|--------------|------------------------|
| **A1** | D ≥ 8 | Player (D) | Majority | **None** | — |
| **A2** | D + I ≥ 8, no C/G needed | Player (D) | Majority w/ inds | **None** (v1) | Aurora-ish; Deeps later may gate I |
| **B1** | C ≥ 8 (home corp) | Corp (default) | None | — | Player not in government |
| **B2** | C ≥ 8 but home corp **lost** majority at election; player holds swing seats | **Offer:** player tolerates corp | Kingmaker | **Commit** bandwidth to home corp IG | **Grant:** large `*_leverage` bump while propping them |
| **C1** | D + C ≥ 8 (non-home C or post-toleration) | Player | Minority + corp deal | **Commit** to each paid corp IG | Optional if already in B2 toleration |
| **C2** | D + G ≥ 8 | Player | Minority + gang deal | **Commit** to paid syndicate IG(s) | Anti-crime path easier if no corp in coalition |
| **C3** | D + C + G ≥ 8 (mixed) | Player | Broad ugly coalition | **Commit** to **each** paid IG separately | Expensive; corps may reprisal on anti-crime |
| **D1** | No combo ≥ 8 even if player offers B2 toleration | **Anarchy** | None | — | Corp home district hurt; syndicates gain |
| **D2** | Player can't afford committed bandwidth | **Collapse** → re-resolve | Was governing | Freed per IG | Triggers calendar update |

**Checker order (deterministic):** A1/A2 → B2 offer (if eligible) → C* if player negotiates control → else B1 if C≥8 → else D1.

---

### Table 2 — City executive (150 seats)

| ID | Condition | Who governs | Notes |
|----|-----------|-------------|-------|
| **E0** | Year 0–1 after tether snap | Player (forced) | Grace: reprisal > leverage OK; bandwidth checks **paused** for removal |
| **E1** | Grace expired; player holds bandwidth for ≥76 via C/G toleration deals | Player | Same C1/C2 logic as districts, scaled to exec seat shares |
| **E2** | Grace expired; insufficient bandwidth | **Corp flunky** (pre-snap figure) | Not a full corp coalition sim — default seat math |
| **E3** | Player voluntarily drops / swaps | Re-run E1 or E2 | “Free bandwidth” by exiting toleration elsewhere |

District anarchy (D1) does **not** apply to city exec in your doc — good to keep that explicit in the table footnote.

---

### Table 3 — Reform vote gates (district or city)

| Reform type | Vote coalition required | Post-vote maintenance | Special |
|-------------|-------------------------|----------------------|---------|
| **Minor** (judiciary, etc.) | Any active **control** template (A/C/E) | No reprisal spike | Baseline perk of holding gavel |
| **Pro-worker** | Control template; target = home corp in district | Bandwidth after **+reprisal** on target (+ other corps unless at war) | Shifts prosperity / money to workers + Docks |
| **Anti-crime** | Control template; prefer **C2** (no gang dependence) | Bandwidth after syndicate reprisal | Corps skip reprisal if not relying on G |
| **Pro-democratic** | See dual-path table below | Large reprisal all IGs; must hold until **next election** | Electorate shift (e.g. drone abstention ends) |

**Pro-democratic dual path** — your line 23–26 reads like both paths must be **available** before the vote (high bar, intentional). Clarify in code as AND vs OR:

| Path | Seat block | Excluded | Typical payee IG |
|------|------------|----------|------------------|
| **P-G** | G + I (+ D filler) ≥ 8 | No C, no H | Families / Edges / Sharks (bandwidth) |
| **P-C** | non-home C + I (+ D filler) ≥ 8 | No G, no H | Magrail/Brazos in Vats, etc. |

If **AND**: council must admit both majorities simultaneously (only works with large I/D). If **OR**: player picks which coalition passes the bill — simpler to implement.

---

### Table 4 — Bandwidth cost sketch (tune later)

| Attitude band | Approx. free bandwidth per IG | Control commit (placeholder) |
|---------------|--------------------------------|------------------------------|
| contempt | 0 | Can't buy support |
| wary | 0–1 | Greyed out |
| transactional | 1–3 | `ceil(seats_provided / 5)` per IG? |
| tolerating | 4–6 | |
| captive | 7+ | |

**Rule:** `free_bandwidth(ig) = max(0, leverage(ig) − reprisal(ig) − committed(ig))`. Reform adds reprisal → shrinks free bandwidth globally for that IG without spending leverage.

**Toleration grant (B2):** one-time `+L` (or lump on enter) while `committed(home_corp) ≥ f(swing_seats)` — separate from the monthly attitude label.

---

### Worked example — hung **Vats** (15 seats)

| C | D | G | H | I |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | 4 | 2 | 1 | 2 |

- No outright majority (B1 fails).
- Soylent home corp **lost** majority → **B2 eligible** if player’s 4 D + 2 I can bridge C to 8 (6+2=8 with I, or 6+4=10 with D — need rule: do D seats count toward corp majority when “offering toleration,” or only uncommitted I/G?).
- If player props Soylent: commit Soylent bandwidth; gain Soylent leverage chunk.
- Player self-control via **C1** (D+C): also need Soylent bandwidth unless already in B2.
- **P-C** for pro-democratic: non-home C = Magrail+Brazos share of C seats only — needs attribution of C seats to IGs (v1 hack: all C in Vats counts as home Soylent for P-C exclusion).

That last row is a **design gap**: pro-democratic “non-home corp” needs either seat tagging or district-level corp identity per C seat.

---

### Small doc nits from this pass

1. **Line 48** — “can't use leverage with Sharks to get Families support” is clear; add that **each reform payee is the IG whose party seats you're relying on**, not the party abstractly.
2. **Grace year (line 52)** — “immediately negotiate” implies a **blocking prompt** at month 13; worth one phrase: event vs silent flip.
3. **Aurora (line 56)** — good “desperation only” fence; pairs with A1 being free bandwidth for most of early game.

## [2026-06-09T01:22:29Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Your clarifications tighten three things at once: what counts as a valid **pro-democratic unlock**, what **attitude** actually means mechanically, and how **toleration** interacts with D seats.

---

### Generalized dual-path rule (replacing P-G / P-C)

Before a pro-democratic reform vote, the checker finds **two distinct majority coalitions** such that:

| Constraint | Meaning |
|------------|---------|
| **Two coalitions** | Both reach majority (8 district / 76 exec) on paper |
| **Disjoint non-D members** | If coalition A uses `{G, I, C, …}`, coalition B uses only parties/IGs **not** in A — **D may appear in both** |
| **Excluded from both** | Home-district corp IG (if any), home-district syndicate IG (if any), **H** |
| **Vote uses one** | Player picks which coalition passes the bill |
| **Pre vs post** | Pre-vote: both coalitions must exist (seat math + affordable bandwidth to **buy** any non-D support). Post-vote: **separate** maintenance check after reprisal spike — the two preview coalitions need **not** still work |

That last split is important narratively: you're proving the reform isn't a one-trick pony (gang-only or outsider-corp-only blackmail), but the reprisal aftermath can look completely different.

**Home IG edge case:** Soylent in Vats will never vote for pro-democratic reform in Vats, so they can't be in either preview coalition. After the spike, though, if you owe them enough leverage (`captive`-tier relationship), they might still **prop up the government** to protect their investment — without having supported the reform. That's a nice distinction: **vote coalition ≠ maintenance coalition**.

---

### Worked example — Vats again (6C 4D 2G 1H 2I)

Home corp = Soylent (C in Vats). H excluded. Non-D must not overlap between paths.

**Path A:** 4D + 2G + 2I = 8 — uses `{G, I}`  
**Path B:** 4D + 4C\* = 8 — uses `{C}` but only **non-home** C counts → need attribution of which C seats are Magrail/Brazos vs Soylent

If all 6 C read as Soylent, Path B fails → pro-democratic blocked until non-home corp poll exists in Vats (organizing/electorate shifts). That's probably intentional.

If 3 Soylent + 3 outsider C: Path B = 4D + 3 outsider C + 1I — can't use I (already in Path A). Need 4D + 4 outsider C or 4D + 3 outsider C + 1D (already have 4D). So 4D + 4 outsider C = 8 works if outsider C ≥ 4.

---

### Wary / contempt → affordability only

Good simplification. Attitude becomes a **label** on `(leverage, reprisal)`; the coalition UI greys out options when:

`free_bandwidth(ig) = max(0, leverage − reprisal − committed) < cost(ig, seats)`

…not when attitude is `wary` or `contempt`. Contempt just means `L − R` is tiny or negative, so you **probably** can't afford it — same outcome, cleaner rule.

---

### D seats count for home-corp toleration

When Soylent loses outright majority in Vats, the toleration offer checks:

> Can player + corp reach ≥ 8, **counting player D seats** toward the corp's governing bloc?

So 6C + 4D = 10 → toleration offer eligible; player props Soylent while extracting leverage. Player D aren't "in coalition with" Soylent narratively, but they count for **keeping home leadership in power** — matches the kingmaker fantasy.

---

### One thing still to pin down

**Syndicate "home district"** for the exclusion rule: Pitts reads as Families/Sharks territory in the sim. Same exclusion as corp home (Families can't be in either preview coalition in Pitts)? I'd assume yes for symmetry.

**Pre-vote bandwidth:** Do both preview coalitions need to be **affordable simultaneously** (committed bandwidth for all payees in A **and** B at once), or only seat-feasible with each path affordable **if chosen**? Simultaneous is much stricter — probably right for "you must have two real options," but worth one sentence in the doc.

---

### Revised checker snippet (conceptual)

```
proDemocraticVotePossible(district):
  homeCorp, homeSynd = homeIgs(district)
  paths = all majorities using parties/IGs excluding {H, homeCorp, homeSynd}
  pairs = (A, B) where nonD(A) ∩ nonD(B) = ∅
  return any pair where seatMath(A) and seatMath(B) and bandwidthCanBuy(A) and bandwidthCanBuy(B)

proDemocraticMaintainAfterReform(district):
  return anyControlCoalitionAffordableAfterReprisalSpike()  // may include home IG
```

---

= Math.min(seatsUsed, ppNum(opts.homeCorpSeatsHeld, seatsUsed))\n                if (homeShare > 0) {\n                    out[homeCorps[0]] = (out[homeCorps[0]] || 0) + homeShare\n                }\n                seatsUsed -= homeShare\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (party === 'G' && homeIgs.length && opts.reformVote) {\n        var homeSynds = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'syndicate' })\n        if (homeSynds.length) {\n            seatsUsed = 0\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (seatsUsed <= 0) { return out }\n\n    var ranked = igList.slice()\n    if (districtId && homeIgs.length) {\n        ranked.sort(function(a, b) {\n            var ah = homeIgs.indexOf(a) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            var bh = homeIgs.indexOf(b) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            return bh - ah\n        })\n    }\n\n    var primary = ranked[0]\n    out[primary] = (out[primary] || 0) + seatsUsed\n    return out\n}\n\nfunction ppMergePayees(target, add) {\n    var keys = Object.keys(add)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        target[ig] = (target[ig] || 0) + add[ig]\n    }\n    return target\n}\n\nfunction ppPayeesToList(payeesByIg) {\n    var list = []\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var seats = payeesByIg[ig]\n        if (seats > 0) {\n            list.push({ ig: ig, seats: seats, cost: ppBandwidthCostForSeats(seats) })\n        }\n    }\n    return list\n}\n\nfunction ppCoalitionAffordable(payeesByIg, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var cost = ppBandwidthCostForSeats(payeesByIg[ig])\n        if (ppFreeBandwidth(ig, opts) < cost) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\nfunction ppNonDPartiesUsed(partyUse) {\n    var used = []\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUse[k], 0) > 0) { used.push(k) }\n    }\n    return used.sort().join('+')\n}\n\nfunction ppPartiesDisjoint(partyUseA, partyUseB) {\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUseA[k], 0) > 0 && ppNum(partyUseB[k], 0) > 0) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\n/**\n * Build one player coalition that uses D plus optional C/G/I blocs to reach majority.\n * partyUse: { C?, G?, I? } seat counts consumed from each bloc.\n */\nfunction ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, partyUse, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var used = { D: dSeats, C: 0, G: 0, I: 0, H: 0 }\n    var payees = {}\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        var want = ppNum(partyUse[k], 0)\n        var have = available[k]\n        if (want <= 0) { continue }\n        if (want > have) { return null }\n        used[k] = want\n        ppMergePayees(payees, ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs(k, want, districtId, opts))\n    }\n\n    var total = used.D + used.C + used.G + used.I\n    if (total < majority) { return null }\n\n    return {\n        id: 'D+' + ppNonDPartiesUsed(used),\n        chamberId: chamberId,\n        districtId: districtId || null,\n        partyUse: { D: used.D, C: used.C, G: used.G, I: used.I },\n        seatTotal: total,\n        payees: ppPayeesToList(payees),\n        affordable: ppCoalitionAffordable(payees, opts),\n    }\n}\n\n/** Minimal seat consumption from each bloc to reach majority given fixed D seats. */\nfunction ppMinimalPartyUse(dSeats, majority, available, pattern) {\n    var need = Math.max(0, majority - dSeats)\n    var use = { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }\n    if (need <= 0) { return use }\n\n    var keys = pattern || ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (need <= 0) { break }\n        var take = Math.min(need, ppNum(available[k], 0))\n        use[k] = take\n        need -= take\n    }\n    if (need > 0) { return null }\n    return use\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var coalitions = []\n    var seen = {}\n\n    if (dSeats >= majority) {\n        var outright = ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }, opts)\n        if (outright) { coalitions.push(outright) }\n    }\n\n    var patterns = [\n        ['C'], ['G'], ['I'],\n        ['C', 'G'], ['C', 'I'], ['G', 'I'],\n        ['C', 'G', 'I'],\n    ]\n\n    for (var p = 0; p < patterns.length; p++) {\n        var use = ppMinimalPartyUse(dSeats, majority, available, patterns[p])\n        if (!use) { continue }\n        var coalition = ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, use, opts)\n        if (!coalition) { continue }\n        if (seen[coalition.id]) { continue }\n        seen[coalition.id] = 1\n        coalitions.push(coalition)\n    }\n\n    return coalitions\n}\n\nfunction ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var homeCorps = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate' })\n    if (!homeCorps.length) { return { possible: false, seatTotal: seats.C } }\n\n    var total = seats.C + seats.D\n    return {\n        possible: total >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY,\n        seatTotal: total,\n        homeCorp: homeCorps[0],\n        corpSeats: seats.C,\n        demSeats: seats.D,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    if (seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY) {\n        return { eligible: false, reason: 'corp_majority' }\n    }\n    var prop = ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId)\n    if (!prop.possible) {\n        return { eligible: false, reason: 'insufficient_seats' }\n    }\n    return {\n        eligible: true,\n        homeCorp: prop.homeCorp,\n        corpSeats: prop.corpSeats,\n        demSeats: prop.demSeats,\n        governingSeats: prop.seatTotal,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckDistrictControl(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, {})\n    var affordable = coalitions.filter(function(c) { return c.affordable })\n    var toleration = ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId)\n    var corpMajority = seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY\n\n    var status = 'anarchy'\n    if (affordable.length) {\n        status = 'player'\n    } else if (corpMajority) {\n        status = 'corp'\n    } else if (toleration.eligible) {\n        status = 'toleration_offer'\n    } else if (ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId).possible) {\n        status = 'corp_with_toleration'\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        seats: seats,\n        status: status,\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        affordableCoalitions: affordable,\n        tolerationOffer: toleration,\n        corpMajority: corpMajority,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckExecutiveControl() {\n    if (ppIsExecutiveGraceActive()) {\n        return {\n            chamberId: 'executive',\n            status: 'player_grace',\n            graceActive: true,\n            coalitions: [],\n            affordableCoalitions: [],\n        }\n    }\n\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('executive', null, {})\n    var affordable = coalitions.filter(function(c) { return c.affordable })\n    return {\n        chamberId: 'executive',\n        status: affordable.length ? 'player' : 'corp_flunky',\n        graceActive: false,\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        affordableCoalitions: affordable,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId) {\n    var opts = { reformVote: true }\n    return ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, opts)\n        .filter(function(c) { return ppNum(c.partyUse.H, 0) === 0 })\n}\n\nfunction ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths(districtId) {\n    var coalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var pairs = []\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        for (var j = i + 1; j < coalitions.length; j++) {\n            var a = coalitions[i]\n            var b = coalitions[j]\n            if (!ppPartiesDisjoint(a.partyUse, b.partyUse)) { continue }\n            if (!a.affordable || !b.affordable) { continue }\n            pairs.push({ pathA: a, pathB: b })\n        }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        homeIgs: ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId),\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        validPairs: pairs,\n        possible: pairs.length > 0,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg) {\n    var delta = reprisalByIg || {}\n    return { reprisalDelta: delta }\n}\n\nfunction ppCanMaintainControl(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (coalitions[i].affordable) { return { ok: true, coalition: coalitions[i] }\n        }\n    }\n    return { ok: false, coalition: null }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckPostReformMaintenance(districtId, reprisalByIg, voteCoalitionId) {\n    var opts = ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg)\n    var maintain = ppCanMaintainControl('district', districtId, opts)\n    var voteCoalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var voteCoalition = null\n    for (var i = 0; i < voteCoalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (voteCoalitions[i].id === voteCoalitionId) {\n            voteCoalition = voteCoalitions[i]\n            break\n        }\n    }\n\n    var voteIgs = {}\n    if (voteCoalition) {\n        for (var j = 0; j < voteCoalition.payees.length; j++) {\n            voteIgs[voteCoalition.payees[j].ig] = 1\n        }\n    }\n\n    var maintenanceIgs = {}\n    if (maintain.ok && maintain.coalition) {\n        for (var k = 0; k < maintain.coalition.payees.length; k++) {\n            maintenanceIgs[maintain.coalition.payees[k].ig] = maintain.coalition.payees[k]\n        }\n    }\n\n    var corpPostReformSupport = false\n    var syndicatePostReformSupport = false\n    var igKeys = Object.keys(maintenanceIgs)\n    for (var m = 0; m < igKeys.length; m++) {\n        var ig = igKeys[m]\n        if (voteIgs[ig]) { continue }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate') { corpPostReformSupport = true }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'syndicate') { syndicatePostReformSupport = true }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        maintained: maintain.ok,\n        maintenanceCoalition: maintain.coalition,\n        voteCoalitionId: voteCoalitionId,\n        achievements: {\n            gotEmByTheShortHairs: corpPostReformSupport,\n            anOfferTheyCantRefuse: syndicatePostReformSupport,\n        },\n    }\n}\n\nQ.ppFreeBandwidth = ppFreeBandwidth\nQ.ppBandwidthCostForSeats = ppBandwidthCostForSeats\nQ.ppHomeIgsForDistrict = ppHomeIgsForDistrict\nQ.ppReadDistrictSeats = ppReadDistrictSeats\nQ.ppReadExecutiveSeats = ppReadExecutiveSeats\nQ.ppIsExecutiveGraceActive = ppIsExecutiveGraceActive\nQ.ppTolerationOfferEligible = ppTolerationOfferEligible\nQ.ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions\nQ.ppCheckDistrictControl = ppCheckDistrictControl\nQ.ppCheckExecutiveControl = ppCheckExecutiveControl\nQ.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths = ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths\nQ.ppCanMaintainControl = ppCanMaintainControl\nQ.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance = ppCheckPostReformMaintenance\n\n// MIKASA_POLITICAL_POWER_RUNTIME_END\n\npolitical_power_runtime_ready = 1\n\n!}\ngo-to: jumpScene\n"

If the player can no longer afford the support they will lose the government (unless they can negotiate a new agreement.)  The leverage needs to be paid for every group whose support you needs and scales by how big a share of the seats they have.\n\nWith control of the government of a district or the city, the player can start implementing pro-worker, pro-democratic and anti-crime reforms.  Passing a reform will usually raise the reprisal of syndicates and/or corporations so you need to have excess support sufficient to maintain your government after the reprisal from the reform.  If you can't maintain the government afterwards you can't get the reform implemented.\n\nPro-worker reforms will shift money from the corporation to the voters living in the corporation's home district and the docks. If you have corporate toleration without dependence on the gangs the corporations wont gain reprisal for you doing anti-crime stuff.\n\nPro-democratic reforms are only possible if the player has enough support to secure a majority both of these ways:\n1) Support from gangs (and independents) without any corporation (or hate) support needed\n2) Support from corporations who dont have the district as their home district (plus independents) without any gang (or hate) support needed.\nSupport for both of these needs to be high enough before the vote.  Additionally you need support high enough to maintain control of the government after the vote (possibly by making a big concession like giving up a government control elsewhere).  Pro democratic reforms will change the electorate (like freeing the drones to vote as they please) and will cause a big reprisal jump for all the gangs and corps.  However if you can maintain control of the government until the next election, suddenly you might find yourself much less depedent on negotiating for support.\n\nBesides these big reforms, just bare control of the government would allow for some smaller stuff like improving the judiciary which wont cause reprisal spikes.  Additionally control of the government would make efforts to improve the police/militia become more effective.\n\nIf the player can't control the government and no corporation has enough votes to control a district government (not city government) even with toleration from the player, there will instead be an anarchy situation where the corporations and gangs start bribing the district politicians left right and center.  This will be very bad for a home district corporation and very good for the syndicates.  If enough governments collapse this way the gangs might become the main threat not the corporations.\n\nThe first step to this is implement a function that checks what, if any coalitions are possible for the player in that district.  This gets checked every time the district votes, allowing the player to negotiate for control if they have the leverage. The player can use the district scene to negotiate for a new coalition at any time, including if they weren't previously controlling the government.\n\nsecuring control with leverage would not lower the leverage but it would use up your \"bandwidth\" of leverage above reprisal.  So if their attitude is transactional and you use 3 leverage to secure support their attitude stays transactional, you just can't use up that leverage elsewhere, you keep using it as long as you are using their support to control the government.  If you voluntarily give up the government or can no longer afford it, that \"bandwidth\" becomes available to use elsewhere.\n\nCalendar: district control will always be updated after a district election or after the player can't afford the government. Increasing vote share increases how much leverage you can get by trading away toleration and reduces how much leverage you need to secure support for your own governments.\n\nThe Hate groups wont ever support you because they are modeled on the Nazi strategy of refusing to accept a minority role in someone elses coalition while planning the violent siezure of power. ## [2026-06-09T01:38:36Z] assistant_thought (afterAgentThought)

I spotted a bug in `ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs` where `nonHome` was always zero because `seatsUsed - seatsUsed` evaluates to zero.

The fix for reform votes treats all C seats in a home district as controlled by the home corporation. Those seats become unavailable to reform coalitions.

The seat-counting fix belongs in `ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions`, not `ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs`. A new `ppReformAvailableSeats` helper will strip home corporations and syndicates from the available seat pool.
```json

  "content": "title: Political power runtime\nmax-visits: 1\nnew-page: false\non-arrival: {!\n\n// MIKASA_POLITICAL_POWER_RUNTIME_BEGIN\n\nvar PP_PARTY_KEYS = ['D', 'I', 'C', 'G', 'H']\nvar PP_DISTRICT_IDS = [\n    'Docks', 'Aurora', 'Vats', 'Railyard', 'Pitts', 'Limelight', 'Deeps',\n]\nvar PP_CORP_IGS = ['soylent', 'magrail', 'brazos']\nvar PP_SYNDICATE_IGS = ['families', 'edges', 'sharks']\nvar PP_IG_IDS = PP_CORP_IGS.concat(PP_SYNDICATE_IGS)\n\nvar PP_DISTRICT_SEATS = 15\nvar PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY = 8\nvar PP_EXEC_SEATS = 150\nvar PP_EXEC_MAJORITY = 76\n/** Seats from one IG's party bloc that consume one bandwidth point. Tune later. */\nvar PP_BANDWIDTH_SEATS_PER_POINT = 5\n\n/**\n * Home IGs for a district (boost >= 1.25 in election_runtime DISTRICT_IG_BOOST).\n * Multiple entries = none vote for pro-democratic reforms in that district.\n */\nvar PP_DISTRICT_HOME_IGS = {\n    Docks: ['magrail'],\n    Aurora: ['edges'],\n    Vats: ['soylent'],\n    Railyard: ['magrail'],\n    Pitts: ['families', 'sharks'],\n    Limelight: ['brazos'],\n    Deeps: [],\n}\n\nvar PP_IG_KIND = {\n    soylent: 'corporate',\n    magrail: 'corporate',\n    brazos: 'corporate',\n    families: 'syndicate',\n    edges: 'syndicate',\n    sharks: 'syndicate',\n}\n\nvar PP_PARTY_IGS = {\n    C: PP_CORP_IGS,\n    G: PP_SYNDICATE_IGS,\n}\n\nfunction ppNum(v, fallback) {\n    var n = Number(v)\n    if (isNaN(n)) { return fallback != null ? fallback : 0 }\n    return n\n}\n\nfunction ppSumSeats(seats) {\n    var total = 0\n    for (var i = 0; i < PP_PARTY_KEYS.length; i++) {\n        total += ppNum(seats[PP_PARTY_KEYS[i]], 0)\n    }\n    return total\n}\n\nfunction ppCloneSeats(seats) {\n    var out = {}\n    for (var i = 0; i < PP_PARTY_KEYS.length; i++) {\n        var k = PP_PARTY_KEYS[i]\n        out[k] = ppNum(seats[k], 0)\n    }\n    return out\n}\n\nfunction ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId) {\n    return {\n        D: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_D'], 0),\n        I: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_I'], 0),\n        C: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_C'], 0),\n        G: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_G'], 0),\n        H: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_H'], 0),\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppReadExecutiveSeats() {\n    return {\n        D: ppNum(Q.Exec_D, 0),\n        I: ppNum(Q.Exec_I, 0),\n        C: ppNum(Q.Exec_C, 0),\n        G: ppNum(Q.Exec_G, 0),\n        H: ppNum(Q.Exec_H, 0),\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId) {\n    return (PP_DISTRICT_HOME_IGS[districtId] || []).slice()\n}\n\nfunction ppCommittedBandwidth(igId) {\n    return ppNum(Q[igId + '_bandwidth_committed'], 0)\n}\n\n/** Free leverage slack for one IG after commitments. Attitude label is informational only. */\nfunction ppFreeBandwidth(igId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var L = ppNum(Q[igId + '_leverage'], 0)\n    var R = ppNum(Q[igId + '_reprisal'], 0)\n    var committed = opts.committed != null ? ppNum(opts.committed, 0) : ppCommittedBandwidth(igId)\n    if (opts.reprisalDelta) {\n        R += ppNum(opts.reprisalDelta[igId], 0)\n    }\n    return Math.max(0, L - R - committed)\n}\n\nfunction ppBandwidthCostForSeats(seatsProvided) {\n    var n = ppNum(seatsProvided, 0)\n    if (n <= 0) { return 0 }\n    return Math.max(1, Math.ceil(n / PP_BANDWIDTH_SEATS_PER_POINT))\n}\n\nfunction ppIsExecutiveGraceActive() {\n    if (ppNum(Q.executive_control_grace_active, 0) === 1) { return true }\n    var untilYear = ppNum(Q.executive_grace_until_year, 0)\n    var untilMonth = ppNum(Q.executive_grace_until_month, 0)\n    if (untilYear <= 0) { return false }\n    var y = ppNum(Q.year, 2300)\n    var m = ppNum(Q.month, 1)\n    return y < untilYear || (y === untilYear && m <= untilMonth)\n}\n\nfunction ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId) {\n    if (chamberId === 'executive') { return PP_EXEC_MAJORITY }\n    return PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY\n}\n\nfunction ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    if (opts.availableSeats) { return ppCloneSeats(opts.availableSeats) }\n    if (chamberId === 'executive') { return ppReadExecutiveSeats() }\n    return ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n}\n\n/** Seat pool for pro-democratic reform vote coalitions (home IGs' blocs excluded). */\nfunction ppReformVoteAvailableSeats(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var hasHomeCorp = false\n    var hasHomeSynd = false\n    for (var i = 0; i < homeIgs.length; i++) {\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[homeIgs[i]] === 'corporate') { hasHomeCorp = true }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[homeIgs[i]] === 'syndicate') { hasHomeSynd = true }\n    }\n    if (hasHomeCorp) { seats.C = 0 }\n    if (hasHomeSynd) { seats.G = 0 }\n    seats.H = 0\n    return seats\n}\n\n/** Attribute party seats to IGs for bandwidth billing (v1 heuristic). */\nfunction ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs(party, seatsUsed, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var igList = PP_PARTY_IGS[party] || []\n    var out = {}\n    if (seatsUsed <= 0 || !igList.length) { return out }\n\n    if (party === 'C' && homeIgs.length && !opts.allowHomeCorpSeats) {\n        var homeCorps = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate' })\n        if (homeCorps.length) {\n            var nonHome = Math.max(0, seatsUsed - seatsUsed)\n            if (opts.reformVote) {\n                seatsUsed = nonHome\n            } else if (opts.tolerationProp) {\n                var homeShare = Math.min(seatsUsed, ppNum(opts.homeCorpSeatsHeld, seatsUsed))\n                if (homeShare > 0) {\n                    out[homeCorps[0]] = (out[homeCorps[0]] || 0) + homeShare\n                }\n                seatsUsed -= homeShare\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (party === 'G' && homeIgs.length && opts.reformVote) {\n        var homeSynds = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'syndicate' })\n        if (homeSynds.length) {\n            seatsUsed = 0\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (seatsUsed <= 0) { return out }\n\n    var ranked = igList.slice()\n    if (districtId && homeIgs.length) {\n        ranked.sort(function(a, b) {\n            var ah = homeIgs.indexOf(a) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            var bh = homeIgs.indexOf(b) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            return bh - ah\n        })\n    }\n\n    var primary = ranked[0]\n    out[primary] = (out[primary] || 0) + seatsUsed\n    return out\n}\n\nfunction ppMergePayees(target, add) {\n    var keys = Object.keys(add)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        target[ig] = (target[ig] || 0) + add[ig]\n    }\n    return target\n}\n\nfunction ppPayeesToList(payeesByIg) {\n    var list = []\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var seats = payeesByIg[ig]\n        if (seats > 0) {\n            list.push({ ig: ig, seats: seats, cost: ppBandwidthCostForSeats(seats) })\n        }\n    }\n    return list\n}\n\nfunction ppCoalitionAffordable(payeesByIg, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var cost = ppBandwidthCostForSeats(payeesByIg[ig])\n        if (ppFreeBandwidth(ig, opts) < cost) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\nfunction ppNonDPartiesUsed(partyUse) {\n    var used = []\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUse[k], 0) > 0) { used.push(k) }\n    }\n    return used.sort().join('+')\n}\n\nfunction ppPartiesDisjoint(partyUseA, partyUseB) {\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUseA[k], 0) > 0 && ppNum(partyUseB[k], 0) > 0) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\n/**\n * Build one player coalition that uses D plus optional C/G/I blocs to reach majority.\n * partyUse: { C?, G?, I? } seat counts consumed from each bloc.\n */\nfunction ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, partyUse, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var used = { D: dSeats, C: 0, G: 0, I: 0, H: 0 }\n    var payees = {}\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        var want = ppNum(partyUse[k], 0)\n        var have = available[k]\n        if (want <= 0) { continue }\n        if (want > have) { return null }\n        used[k] = want\n        ppMergePayees(payees, ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs(k, want, districtId, opts))\n    }\n\n    var total = used.D + used.C + used.G + used.I\n    if (total < majority) { return null }\n\n    return {\n        id: 'D+' + ppNonDPartiesUsed(used),\n        chamberId: chamberId,\n        districtId: districtId || null,\n        partyUse: { D: used.D, C: used.C, G: used.G, I: used.I },\n        seatTotal: total,\n        payees: ppPayeesToList(payees),\n        affordable: ppCoalitionAffordable(payees, opts),\n    }\n}\n\n/** Minimal seat consumption from each bloc to reach majority given fixed D seats. */\nfunction ppMinimalPartyUse(dSeats, majority, available, pattern) {\n    var need = Math.max(0, majority - dSeats)\n    var use = { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }\n    if (need <= 0) { return use }\n\n    var keys = pattern || ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (need <= 0) { break }\n        var take = Math.min(need, ppNum(available[k], 0))\n        use[k] = take\n        need -= take\n    }\n    if (need > 0) { return null }\n    return use\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var coalitions = []\n    var seen = {}\n\n    if (dSeats >= majority) {\n        var outright = ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }, opts)\n        if (outright) { coalitions.push(outright) }\n    }\n\n    var patterns = [\n        ['C'], ['G'], ['I'],\n        ['C', 'G'], ['C', 'I'], ['G', 'I'],\n        ['C', 'G', 'I'],\n    ]\n\n    for (var p = 0; p < patterns.length; p++) {\n        var use = ppMinimalPartyUse(dSeats, majority, available, patterns[p])\n        if (!use) { continue }\n        var coalition = ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, use, opts)\n        if (!coalition) { continue }\n        if (seen[coalition.id]) { continue }\n        seen[coalition.id] = 1\n        coalitions.push(coalition)\n    }\n\n    return coalitions\n}\n\nfunction ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var homeCorps = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate' })\n    if (!homeCorps.length) { return { possible: false, seatTotal: seats.C } }\n\n    var total = seats.C + seats.D\n    return {\n        possible: total >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY,\n        seatTotal: total,\n        homeCorp: homeCorps[0],\n        corpSeats: seats.C,\n        demSeats: seats.D,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    if (seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY) {\n        return { eligible: false, reason: 'corp_majority' }\n    }\n    var prop = ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId)\n    if (!prop.possible) {\n        return { eligible: false, reason: 'insufficient_seats' }\n    }\n    return {\n        eligible: true,\n        homeCorp: prop.homeCorp,\n        corpSeats: prop.corpSeats,\n        demSeats: prop.demSeats,\n        governingSeats: prop.seatTotal,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckDistrictControl(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, {})\n    var affordable = coalitions.filter(function(c) { return c.affordable })\n    var toleration = ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId)\n    var corpMajority = seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY\n\n    var status = 'anarchy'\n    if (affordable.length) {\n        status = 'player'\n    } else if (corpMajority) {\n        status = 'corp'\n    } else if (toleration.eligible) {\n        status = 'toleration_offer'\n    } else if (ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId).possible) {\n        status = 'corp_with_toleration'\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        seats: seats,\n        status: status,\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        affordableCoalitions: affordable,\n        tolerationOffer: toleration,\n        corpMajority: corpMajority,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckExecutiveControl() {\n    if (ppIsExecutiveGraceActive()) {\n        return {\n            chamberId: 'executive',\n            status: 'player_grace',\n            graceActive: true,\n            coalitions: [],\n            affordableCoalitions: [],\n        }\n    }\n\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('executive', null, {})\n    var affordable = coalitions.filter(function(c) { return c.affordable })\n    return {\n        chamberId: 'executive',\n        status: affordable.length ? 'player' : 'corp_flunky',\n        graceActive: false,\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        affordableCoalitions: affordable,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId) {\n    var opts = { reformVote: true }\n    return ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, opts)\n        .filter(function(c) { return ppNum(c.partyUse.H, 0) === 0 })\n}\n\nfunction ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths(districtId) {\n    var coalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var pairs = []\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        for (var j = i + 1; j < coalitions.length; j++) {\n            var a = coalitions[i]\n            var b = coalitions[j]\n            if (!ppPartiesDisjoint(a.partyUse, b.partyUse)) { continue }\n            if (!a.affordable || !b.affordable) { continue }\n            pairs.push({ pathA: a, pathB: b })\n        }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        homeIgs: ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId),\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        validPairs: pairs,\n        possible: pairs.length > 0,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg) {\n    var delta = reprisalByIg || {}\n    return { reprisalDelta: delta }\n}\n\nfunction ppCanMaintainControl(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (coalitions[i].affordable) { return { ok: true, coalition: coalitions[i] }\n        }\n    }\n    return { ok: false, coalition: null }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckPostReformMaintenance(districtId, reprisalByIg, voteCoalitionId) {\n    var opts = ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg)\n    var maintain = ppCanMaintainControl('district', districtId, opts)\n    var voteCoalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var voteCoalition = null\n    for (var i = 0; i < voteCoalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (voteCoalitions[i].id === voteCoalitionId) {\n            voteCoalition = voteCoalitions[i]\n            break\n        }\n    }\n\n    var voteIgs = {}\n    if (voteCoalition) {\n        for (var j = 0; j < voteCoalition.payees.length; j++) {\n            voteIgs[voteCoalition.payees[j].ig] = 1\n        }\n    }\n\n    var maintenanceIgs = {}\n    if (maintain.ok && maintain.coalition) {\n        for (var k = 0; k < maintain.coalition.payees.length; k++) {\n            maintenanceIgs[maintain.coalition.payees[k].ig] = maintain.coalition.payees[k]\n        }\n    }\n\n    var corpPostReformSupport = false\n    var syndicatePostReformSupport = false\n    var igKeys = Object.keys(maintenanceIgs)\n    for (var m = 0; m < igKeys.length; m++) {\n        var ig = igKeys[m]\n        if (voteIgs[ig]) { continue }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate') { corpPostReformSupport = true }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'syndicate') { syndicatePostReformSupport = true }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        maintained: maintain.ok,\n        maintenanceCoalition: maintain.coalition,\n        voteCoalitionId: voteCoalitionId,\n        achievements: {\n            gotEmByTheShortHairs: corpPostReformSupport,\n            anOfferTheyCantRefuse: syndicatePostReformSupport,\n        },\n    }\n}\n\nQ.ppFreeBandwidth = ppFreeBandwidth\nQ.ppBandwidthCostForSeats = ppBandwidthCostForSeats\nQ.ppHomeIgsForDistrict = ppHomeIgsForDistrict\nQ.ppReadDistrictSeats = ppReadDistrictSeats\nQ.ppReadExecutiveSeats = ppReadExecutiveSeats\nQ.ppIsExecutiveGraceActive = ppIsExecutiveGraceActive\nQ.ppTolerationOfferEligible = ppTolerationOfferEligible\nQ.ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions\nQ.ppCheckDistrictControl = ppCheckDistrictControl\nQ.ppCheckExecutiveControl = ppCheckExecutiveControl\nQ.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths = ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths\nQ.ppCanMaintainControl = ppCanMaintainControl\nQ.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance = ppCheckPostReformMaintenance\n\n// MIKASA_POLITICAL_POWER_RUNTIME_END\n\npolitical_power_runtime_ready = 1\n\n!}\ngo-to: jumpScene\n"
}

i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (coalitions[i].affordable) { return { ok: true, coalition: coalitions[i] }\n        }\n    }\n    return { ok: false, coalition: null }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckPostReformMaintenance(districtId, reprisalByIg, voteCoalitionId) {\n    var opts = ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg)\n    var maintain = ppCanMaintainControl('district', districtId, opts)\n    var voteCoalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var voteCoalition = null\n    for (var i = 0; i < voteCoalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (voteCoalitions[i].id === voteCoalitionId) {\n            voteCoalition = voteCoalitions[i]\n            break\n        }\n    }\n\n    var voteIgs = {}\n    if (voteCoalition) {\n        for (var j = 0; j < voteCoalition.payees.length; j++) {\n            voteIgs[voteCoalition.payees[j].ig] = 1\n        }\n    }\n\n    var maintenanceIgs = {}\n    if (maintain.ok && maintain.coalition) {\n        for (var k = 0; k < maintain.coalition.payees.length; k++) {\n            maintenanceIgs[maintain.coalition.payees[k].ig] = maintain.coalition.payees[k]\n        }\n    }\n\n    var corpPostReformSupport = false\n    var syndicatePostReformSupport = false\n    var igKeys = Object.keys(maintenanceIgs)\n    for (var m = 0; m < igKeys.length; m++) {\n        var ig = igKeys[m]\n        if (voteIgs[ig]) { continue }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate') { corpPostReformSupport = true }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'syndicate') { syndicatePostReformSupport = true }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        maintained: maintain.ok,\n        maintenanceCoalition: maintain.coalition,\n        voteCoalitionId: voteCoalitionId,\n        achievements: {\n            gotEmByTheShortHairs: corpPostReformSupport,\n            anOfferTheyCantRefuse: syndicatePostReformSupport,\n        },\n    }\n}\n\nQ.ppFreeBandwidth = ppFreeBandwidth\nQ.ppBandwidthCostForSeats = ppBandwidthCostForSeats\nQ.ppHomeIgsForDistrict = ppHomeIgsForDistrict\nQ.ppReadDistrictSeats = ppReadDistrictSeats\nQ.ppReadExecutiveSeats = ppReadExecutiveSeats\nQ.ppIsExecutiveGraceActive = ppIsExecutiveGraceActive\nQ.ppTolerationOfferEligible = ppTolerationOfferEligible\nQ.ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions\nQ.ppCheckDistrictControl = ppCheckDistrictControl\nQ.ppCheckExecutiveControl = ppCheckExecutiveControl\nQ.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths = ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths\nQ.ppCanMaintainControl = ppCanMaintainControl\nQ.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance = ppCheckPostReformMaintenance\n\n// MIKASA_POLITICAL_POWER_RUNTIME_END\n\npolitical_power_runtime_ready = 1\n\n!}\ngo-to: jumpScene\n"
}
```

## [2026-06-09T01:38:53Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Updated `planning/political_power.md` with the dual-path rules, home-IG reform exclusion, attitude vs bandwidth, toleration headcount, achievement stubs, and an implementation pointer.

Added **`source/scenes/political_power_runtime.scene.dry`** — not wired into `setup_functions` or district scenes yet. Build compiles cleanly.

### API surface (`Q.pp*`)

| Function | Role |
|----------|------|
| `ppFreeBandwidth(igId, opts?)` | `leverage − reprisal − committed`; optional `reprisalDelta` for post-reform checks |
| `ppBandwidthCostForSeats(n)` | `ceil(n / 5)`, min 1 |
| `ppHomeIgsForDistrict(id)` | Home IGs from static table (Pitts = families + sharks, etc.) |
| `ppReadDistrictSeats` / `ppReadExecutiveSeats` | Read `Vats_C`, `Exec_D`, … |
| `ppIsExecutiveGraceActive()` | `executive_control_grace_active` or until year/month |
| `ppTolerationOfferEligible(id)` | Home corp lost C majority; D seats count toward prop |
| `ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamber, district?, opts?)` | All D-based majorities with payee list + `affordable` flag |
| `ppCheckDistrictControl(id)` | `{ status, coalitions, affordableCoalitions, tolerationOffer }` |
| `ppCheckExecutiveControl()` | Grace → `player_grace`; else player vs `corp_flunky` |
| `ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths(id)` | Disjoint non-D pairs; home IGs' C/G blocs zeroed for reform pool |
| `ppCanMaintainControl(chamber, district?, opts?)` | Any affordable coalition after reprisal delta |
| `ppCheckPostReformMaintenance(id, reprisalByIg, voteCoalitionId)` | Maintenance + achievement hints |

**District `status` values:** `player` | `corp` | `toleration_offer` | `corp_with_toleration` | `anarchy`

**Achievement hints** (not unlocked yet — just flags on post-reform check):

```javascript
achievements: {
  gotEmByTheShortHairs: true,      // corp in maintenance, not in vote coalition
  anOfferTheyCantRefuse: true,     // syndicate same
}
```

### v1 stubs to tune when wiring

- **`PP_BANDWIDTH_SEATS_PER_POINT = 5`** — placeholder cost scaling
- **C/G seat split** — in a home district, all C (or G if home syndicates) is unavailable for reform vote coalitions; post-reform maintenance uses full seat pool
- **`Q[ig + '_bandwidth_committed']`** — expected for committed bandwidth (defaults 0)
- **IG payee attribution** — all consumed C/G seats bill to one primary IG per party for now

When you're ready to wire up, the bootstrap change is a few lines in `setup_functions.scene.dry` plus calling `ppCheckDistrictControl` from district scenes / election tick.
