{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/planning/political_power.md",
  "content": "Making the elections actually matter while keeping to the theme of corporate oligarchy will take a little bit of finesse.  The important part will be securing leverage even without a majority coalition.  There are multiple dynamics in play:\n\n1. The Aurora district is an outlier where the democrats control it from the start of the game, the player just needs to keep from losing it.\n2. The Deeps is a special case where it's about winning the trust of the Deepers (something to tackle later)\n3. The Pitts and the Docks are a bit strange so the player might have lots of support there or none at all.  But even there the player will mostly want to leverage point 4.\n4. The real important dynamic is in the corporate districts and the citywide government.  Here it's all about securing leverage by making sure nobody else has a clean majority, letting you trade away toleration agreements for leverage.\n\n\nIn the corporate home districts the corporations have a huge advantage because of the drones vote.  But if the drones start abstaining en masse and no longer constitute a majority of voters the vote shifts to the 1/2/3rd waves and coops, much less corporation friendly demographics.  \n\nIf a coorporation is knocked out of a majority in it's home district and the player has enough seats to give them a majority, they will be given the option to offer toleration to current district leadership.  This results in a big chunk of leverage on the corporation which lasts as long as the player is keeping them in power.  Losing control of their home district government should be a big blow to the corps.  \n\nWith the city government, the player gets a free year of control after the tether snap event but after that they can continue to control the government if they have enough leverage on corps/syndicates to buy a majority.  If they dont, a corporate flunky is put back in charge.  The player could also negotiate toleration like with district governments.\n\nTo control a district government (without winning an outright majority), the player will need to have leverage to cash in with corporations and syndicates.  The player needs to maintain a leverage over reprisal surplus of a certain size to afford support for control of a district or the city.  If the player can no longer afford the support they will lose the government (unless they can negotiate a new agreement.)  The leverage needs to be paid for every group whose support you needs and scales by how big a share of the seats they have.\n\nWith control of the government of a district or the city, the player can start implementing pro-worker, pro-democratic and anti-crime reforms.  Passing a reform will usually raise the reprisal of syndicates and/or corporations so you need to have excess support sufficient to maintain your government after the reprisal from the reform.  If you can't maintain the government afterwards you can't get the reform implemented.\n\nPro-worker reforms will shift money from the corporation to the voters living in the corporation's home district and the docks.  This will weaken the corporation as well as give reprisal.  The other corps will also gain reprisal unless they are at war with the target.\n\nAnti-crime reforms likewise weaken the syndicates and give reprisal but unlike the corporations the syndicates mostly dont have a stranglehold on a district government.  If you have corporate toleration without dependence on the gangs the corporations wont gain reprisal for you doing anti-crime stuff.\n\nPro-democratic reforms are only possible if the player has enough support to secure a majority both of these ways:\n1) Support from gangs (and independents) without any corporation (or hate) support needed\n2) Support from corporations who dont have the district as their home district (plus independents) without any gang (or hate) support needed.\nSupport for both of these needs to be high enough before the vote.  Additionally you need support high enough to maintain control of the government after the vote (possibly by making a big concession like giving up a government control elsewhere).  Pro democratic reforms will change the electorate (like freeing the drones to vote as they please) and will cause a big reprisal jump for all the gangs and corps.  However if you can maintain control of the government until the next election, suddenly you might find yourself much less depedent on negotiating for support.\n\nBesides these big reforms, just bare control of the government would allow for some smaller stuff like improving the judiciary which wont cause reprisal spikes.  Additionally control of the government would make efforts to improve the police/militia become more effective.\n\nIf the player can't control the government and no corporation has enough votes to control a district government even with toleration from the player, there will instead be an anarchy situation where the corporations and gangs start bribing the district politicians left right and center.  This will be very bad for a home district corporation and very good for the syndicates.  If enough governments collapse this way the gangs might become the main threat not the corporations.  **Exception: the Docks** — like citywide government, the fallback is a **corporate flunky from before** (pre-tether default leadership), not anarchy. District anarchy applies only to the other districts.\n\nThe first step to this is implement a function that checks what, if any coalitions are possible for the player in that district.  This gets checked every time the district votes, allowing the player to negotiate for control if they have the leverage.  If a coalition is possible but the leverage isn't there, it will appear greyed out.  The player can use the district scene to negotiate for a new coalition at any time, including if they weren't previously controlling the government.\n\nsecuring control with leverage would not lower the leverage but it would use up your \"bandwidth\" of leverage above reprisal.  So if their attitude is transactional and you use 3 leverage to secure support their attitude stays transactional, you just can't use up that leverage elsewhere, you keep using it as long as you are using their support to control the government.  If you voluntarily give up the government or can no longer afford it, that \"bandwidth\" becomes available to use elsewhere.\n\nCalendar: district control will always be updated after a district election or after the player can't afford the government.  The player can also use the district menu to update this sooner.\n\nThe free year after tether snap is for control of the city government.  The corporations need to leave you in government at least that long to placate the non-Mikasa corporations.  Even if reprisal exceeds leverage they will leave you in place for the one year grace period.  After that yearlong grace period you'll need to negotiate for control again.  You are forced to renegotiate for control if you no longer have enough leverage \"bandwidth\" to control the government.  The player could also voluntarily negotiate for control (not sure how this will be triggered).\n\nThe leverage loop is heavily influenced by the fact that trading away toleration is the easiest way to get leverage.  The major phases will result in some political shifts but mostly in order to have control somewhere you need to tolerate somewhere else.  Increasing vote share increases how much leverage you can get by trading away toleration and reduces how much leverage you need to secure support for your own governments.\n\nThe Hate groups wont ever support you because they are modeled on the Nazi strategy of refusing to accept a minority role in someone elses coalition while planning the violent siezure of power.  So keeping their vote share down is important because any seats they hold are unavailable for making deals.\n\nI'm not covering any possibilities besides toleration, control and anarchy because I'm afraid of scope creep.  I'm going to assume that generally the corps and gangs are at each other's throats so much that the player can always negotiate a toleration agreement with them.  If the corporations or gangs are more afraid of the player then of each other then you will be rapidly arriving at the endgame where they go to war with you.\n\nCovering the loss of control in Aurora is something I think can be left for later.\n\nbandwidth is per group.  If you need the support from the 7 families, you can't use leverage with the Sharks to get that support.\n\nThe reforms shrink the bandwidth because they raise reprisal.  Bandwidth is just the term I was using for the gap between leverage and reprisal.\n\nAt the grace year expiration, the player would need to immediately negotiate for control of the government or the corporations will agree on putting a \"neutral\" corporate flunky in charge, this is the same figure who was in charge before the tether snap incident.\n\nVoluntarily negotiating for control is about getting leverage by going to toleration or swapping support because you need to free up leverage with a group.\n\nIf Aurora toleration is implemented it should be something of a move of desperation, a corporation or syndicate in control of Aurora will do tons of damage at a very fast pace.\n\n## Pro-democratic dual path (general rule)\n\nBefore a pro-democratic reform vote, two distinct majority coalitions must exist:\n\n- Both reach majority on seat math; the player only **uses one** to pass the bill.\n- Non-D members must be **disjoint** between the two paths (D may appear in both).\n- **H** is excluded from both paths.\n- Any **home-district IG** (corp or syndicate) is excluded from both paths — they will not vote for pro-democratic reforms in their home territory. If multiple IGs call a district home (e.g. Pitts), **none** of them vote for reforms there.\n- Pre-vote: both paths must be affordable (bandwidth to buy non-D support). Post-vote: separate maintenance check after the reprisal spike — the preview paths need not still work afterward.\n- Edge case: a home corp/syndicate may still **prop up the government** after the reform if leverage is high enough (they protect their investment), even though they never voted for the reform. Vote coalition ≠ maintenance coalition.\n\n## Attitude vs affordability\n\n`wary` or `contempt` attitude does **not** block buying support — it usually means `leverage − reprisal` is too small to afford the bandwidth cost. Grey out options on bandwidth, not attitude label.\n\n## Toleration headcount\n\nWhen offering toleration to a home corp that lost its outright majority, **Dem seats count** toward the bloc keeping that leadership in power (e.g. 6C + 4D reaches 10).\n\n## Achievements (add later)\n\n| Title | Icon | Unlock |\n|-------|------|--------|\n| Got 'em by the short hairs. | clothespin | After a pro-democratic reform, a **corporation** supports your government in the post-reform maintenance check without having been in the reform vote coalition. |\n| An offer they can't refuse. | cottonball | Same, but a **syndicate**. |\n\n## Implementation\n\nDraft control-check API (not wired into boot/tick yet): `source/scenes/political_power_runtime.scene.dry` — `Q.ppCheckDistrictControl`, `Q.ppCheckExecutiveControl`, `Q.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths`, `Q.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance`, etc."
}
```

Bandwidth is just the term I was using for the gap between leverage and reprisal.\n\nAt the grace year expiration, the player would need to immediately negotiate for control of the government or the corporations will agree on putting a \"neutral\" corporate flunky in charge, this is the same figure who was in charge before the tether snap incident.\n\nVoluntarily negotiating for control is about getting leverage by going to toleration or swapping support because you need to free up leverage with a group.\n\nIf Aurora toleration is implemented it should be something of a move of desperation, a corporation or syndicate in control of Aurora will do tons of damage at a very fast pace.\n\n## Pro-democratic dual path (general rule)\n\nBefore a pro-democratic reform vote, two distinct majority coalitions must exist:\n\n- Both reach majority on seat math; the player only **uses one** to pass the bill.\n- Non-D members must be **disjoint** between the two paths (D may appear in both).\n- **H** is excluded from both paths.\n- Any **home-district IG** (corp or syndicate) is excluded from both paths — they will not vote for pro-democratic reforms in their home territory. If multiple IGs call a district home (e.g. Pitts), **none** of them vote for reforms there.\n- Pre-vote: both paths must be affordable (bandwidth to buy non-D support). Post-vote: separate maintenance check after the reprisal spike — the preview paths need not still work afterward.\n- Edge case: a home corp/syndicate may still **prop up the government** after the reform if leverage is high enough (they protect their investment), even though they never voted for the reform. Vote coalition ≠ maintenance coalition.\n\n## Attitude vs affordability\n\n`wary` or `contempt` attitude does **not** block buying support — it usually means `leverage − reprisal` is too small to afford the bandwidth cost. Grey out options on bandwidth, not attitude label.\n\n## Toleration headcount\n\nWhen offering toleration to a home corp that lost its outright majority, **Dem seats count** toward the bloc keeping that leadership in power (e.g. 6C + 4D reaches 10).\n\n## Achievements (add later)\n\n| Title | Icon | Unlock |\n|-------|------|--------|\n| Got 'em by the short hairs. | clothespin | After a pro-democratic reform, a **corporation** supports your government in the post-reform maintenance check without having been in the reform vote coalition. |\n| An offer they can't refuse. | cottonball | Same, but a **syndicate**. |\n\n## Implementation\n\nDraft control-check API (not wired into boot/tick yet): `source/scenes/political_power_runtime.scene.dry` — `Q.ppCheckDistrictControl`, `Q.ppCheckExecutiveControl`, `Q.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths`, `Q.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance`, etc."
}

Tune later. */\nvar PP_BANDWIDTH_SEATS_PER_POINT = 5\n\n/**\n * Home IGs for a district (boost >= 1.25 in election_runtime DISTRICT_IG_BOOST).\n * Multiple entries = none vote for pro-democratic reforms in that district.\n */\nvar PP_DISTRICT_HOME_IGS = {\n    Docks: ['magrail'],\n    Aurora: ['edges'],\n    Vats: ['soylent'],\n    Railyard: ['magrail'],\n    Pitts: ['families', 'sharks'],\n    Limelight: ['brazos'],\n    Deeps: [],\n}\n\nvar PP_IG_KIND = {\n    soylent: 'corporate',\n    magrail: 'corporate',\n    brazos: 'corporate',\n    families: 'syndicate',\n    edges: 'syndicate',\n    sharks: 'syndicate',\n}\n\nvar PP_PARTY_IGS = {\n    C: PP_CORP_IGS,\n    G: PP_SYNDICATE_IGS,\n}\n\nfunction ppNum(v, fallback) {\n    var n = Number(v)\n    if (isNaN(n)) { return fallback != null ? fallback : 0 }\n    return n\n}\n\nfunction ppSumSeats(seats) {\n    var total = 0\n    for (var i = 0; i < PP_PARTY_KEYS.length; i++) {\n        total += ppNum(seats[PP_PARTY_KEYS[i]], 0)\n    }\n    return total\n}\n\nfunction ppCloneSeats(seats) {\n    var out = {}\n    for (var i = 0; i < PP_PARTY_KEYS.length; i++) {\n        var k = PP_PARTY_KEYS[i]\n        out[k] = ppNum(seats[k], 0)\n    }\n    return out\n}\n\nfunction ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId) {\n    return {\n        D: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_D'], 0),\n        I: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_I'], 0),\n        C: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_C'], 0),\n        G: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_G'], 0),\n        H: ppNum(Q[districtId + '_H'], 0),\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppReadExecutiveSeats() {\n    return {\n        D: ppNum(Q.Exec_D, 0),\n        I: ppNum(Q.Exec_I, 0),\n        C: ppNum(Q.Exec_C, 0),\n        G: ppNum(Q.Exec_G, 0),\n        H: ppNum(Q.Exec_H, 0),\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId) {\n    return (PP_DISTRICT_HOME_IGS[districtId] || []).slice()\n}\n\nfunction ppCommittedBandwidth(igId) {\n    return ppNum(Q[igId + '_bandwidth_committed'], 0)\n}\n\n/** Free leverage slack for one IG after commitments. Attitude label is informational only. */\nfunction ppFreeBandwidth(igId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var L = ppNum(Q[igId + '_leverage'], 0)\n    var R = ppNum(Q[igId + '_reprisal'], 0)\n    var committed = opts.committed != null ? ppNum(opts.committed, 0) : ppCommittedBandwidth(igId)\n    if (opts.reprisalDelta) {\n        R += ppNum(opts.reprisalDelta[igId], 0)\n    }\n    return Math.max(0, L - R - committed)\n}\n\nfunction ppBandwidthCostForSeats(seatsProvided) {\n    var n = ppNum(seatsProvided, 0)\n    if (n <= 0) { return 0 }\n    return Math.max(1, Math.ceil(n / PP_BANDWIDTH_SEATS_PER_POINT))\n}\n\nfunction ppIsExecutiveGraceActive() {\n    if (ppNum(Q.executive_control_grace_active, 0) === 1) { return true }\n    var untilYear = ppNum(Q.executive_grace_until_year, 0)\n    var untilMonth = ppNum(Q.executive_grace_until_month, 0)\n    if (untilYear <= 0) { return false }\n    var y = ppNum(Q.year, 2300)\n    var m = ppNum(Q.month, 1)\n    return y < untilYear || (y === untilYear && m <= untilMonth)\n}\n\nfunction ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId) {\n    if (chamberId === 'executive') { return PP_EXEC_MAJORITY }\n    return PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY\n}\n\nfunction ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    if (opts.availableSeats) { return ppCloneSeats(opts.availableSeats) }\n    if (chamberId === 'executive') { return ppReadExecutiveSeats() }\n    return ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n}\n\n/** Seat pool for pro-democratic reform vote coalitions (home IGs' blocs excluded). */\nfunction ppReformVoteAvailableSeats(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var hasHomeCorp = false\n    var hasHomeSynd = false\n    for (var i = 0; i < homeIgs.length; i++) {\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[homeIgs[i]] === 'corporate') { hasHomeCorp = true }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[homeIgs[i]] === 'syndicate') { hasHomeSynd = true }\n    }\n    if (hasHomeCorp) { seats.C = 0 }\n    if (hasHomeSynd) { seats.G = 0 }\n    seats.H = 0\n    return seats\n}\n\n/** Attribute party seats to IGs for bandwidth billing (v1 heuristic). */\nfunction ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs(party, seatsUsed, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var igList = PP_PARTY_IGS[party] || []\n    var out = {}\n    if (seatsUsed <= 0 || !igList.length) { return out }\n\n    if (party === 'C' && homeIgs.length && opts.reformVote) {\n        return out\n    }\n\n    if (party === 'G' && homeIgs.length && opts.reformVote) {\n        return out\n    }\n\n    if (party === 'C' && homeIgs.length && opts.tolerationProp) {\n        var homeCorpsOnly = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate' })\n        if (homeCorpsOnly.length) {\n            var homeShare = Math.min(seatsUsed, ppNum(opts.homeCorpSeatsHeld, seatsUsed))\n            if (homeShare > 0) {\n                out[homeCorpsOnly[0]] = (out[homeCorpsOnly[0]] || 0) + homeShare\n            }\n            seatsUsed -= homeShare\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (seatsUsed <= 0) { return out }\n\n    var ranked = igList.slice()\n    if (districtId && homeIgs.length) {\n        ranked.sort(function(a, b) {\n            var ah = homeIgs.indexOf(a) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            var bh = homeIgs.indexOf(b) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            return bh - ah\n        })\n    }\n\n    var primary = ranked[0]\n    out[primary] = (out[primary] || 0) + seatsUsed\n    return out\n}\n\nfunction ppMergePayees(target, add) {\n    var keys = Object.keys(add)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        target[ig] = (target[ig] || 0) + add[ig]\n    }\n    return target\n}\n\nfunction ppPayeesToList(payeesByIg) {\n    var list = []\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var seats = payeesByIg[ig]\n        if (seats > 0) {\n            list.push({ ig: ig, seats: seats, cost: ppBandwidthCostForSeats(seats) })\n        }\n    }\n    return list\n}\n\nfunction ppCoalitionAffordable(payeesByIg, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var cost = ppBandwidthCostForSeats(payeesByIg[ig])\n        if (ppFreeBandwidth(ig, opts) < cost) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\nfunction ppNonDPartiesUsed(partyUse) {\n    var used = []\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUse[k], 0) > 0) { used.push(k) }\n    }\n    return used.sort().join('+')\n}\n\nfunction ppPartiesDisjoint(partyUseA, partyUseB) {\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUseA[k], 0) > 0 && ppNum(partyUseB[k], 0) > 0) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\n/**\n * Build one player coalition that uses D plus optional C/G/I blocs to reach majority.\n * partyUse: { C?, G?, I? } seat counts consumed from each bloc.\n */\nfunction ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, partyUse, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var used = { D: dSeats, C: 0, G: 0, I: 0, H: 0 }\n    var payees = {}\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        var want = ppNum(partyUse[k], 0)\n        var have = available[k]\n        if (want <= 0) { continue }\n        if (want > have) { return null }\n        used[k] = want\n        ppMergePayees(payees, ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs(k, want, districtId, opts))\n    }\n\n    var total = used.D + used.C + used.G + used.I\n    if (total < majority) { return null }\n\n    return {\n        id: 'D+' + ppNonDPartiesUsed(used),\n        chamberId: chamberId,\n        districtId: districtId || null,\n        partyUse: { D: used.D, C: used.C, G: used.G, I: used.I },\n        seatTotal: total,\n        payees: ppPayeesToList(payees),\n        affordable: ppCoalitionAffordable(payees, opts),\n    }\n}\n\n/** Minimal seat consumption from each bloc to reach majority given fixed D seats. */\nfunction ppMinimalPartyUse(dSeats, majority, available, pattern) {\n    var need = Math.max(0, majority - dSeats)\n    var use = { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }\n    if (need <= 0) { return use }\n\n    var keys = pattern || ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (need <= 0) { break }\n        var take = Math.min(need, ppNum(available[k], 0))\n        use[k] = take\n        need -= take\n    }\n    if (need > 0) { return null }\n    return use\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var coalitions = []\n    var seen = {}\n\n    if (dSeats >= majority) {\n        var outright = ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }, opts)\n        if (outright) { coalitions.push(outright) }\n    }\n\n    var patterns = [\n        ['C'], ['G'], ['I'],\n        ['C', 'G'], ['C', 'I'], ['G', 'I'],\n        ['C', 'G', 'I'],\n    ]\n\n    for (var p = 0; p < patterns.length; p++) {\n        var use = ppMinimalPartyUse(dSeats, majority, available, patterns[p])\n        if (!use) { continue }\n        var coalition = ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, use, opts)\n        if (!coalition) { continue }\n        if (seen[coalition.id]) { continue }\n        seen[coalition.id] = 1\n        coalitions.push(coalition)\n    }\n\n    return coalitions\n}\n\nfunction ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var homeCorps = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate' })\n    if (!homeCorps.length) { return { possible: false, seatTotal: seats.C } }\n\n    var total = seats.C + seats.D\n    return {\n        possible: total >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY,\n        seatTotal: total,\n        homeCorp: homeCorps[0],\n        corpSeats: seats.C,\n        demSeats: seats.D,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    if (seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY) {\n        return { eligible: false, reason: 'corp_majority' }\n    }\n    var prop = ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId)\n    if (!prop.possible) {\n        return { eligible: false, reason: 'insufficient_seats' }\n    }\n    return {\n        eligible: true,\n        homeCorp: prop.homeCorp,\n        corpSeats: prop.corpSeats,\n        demSeats: prop.demSeats,\n        governingSeats: prop.seatTotal,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppDistrictFailureFallback(districtId) {\n    if (districtId === PP_CORP_FLUNKY_DISTRICT) { return 'corp_flunky' }\n    return 'anarchy'\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckDistrictControl(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, {})\n    var affordable = coalitions.filter(function(c) { return c.affordable })\n    var toleration = ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId)\n    var corpMajority = seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY\n\n    var status = ppDistrictFailureFallback(districtId)\n    if (affordable.length) {\n        status = 'player'\n    } else if (corpMajority) {\n        status = 'corp'\n    } else if (toleration.eligible) {\n        status = 'toleration_offer'\n    } else if (ppCorpCanGovernWithToleration(districtId).possible) {\n        status = 'corp_with_toleration'\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        seats: seats,\n        status: status,\n        failureFallback: ppDistrictFailureFallback(districtId),\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        affordableCoalitions: affordable,\n        tolerationOffer: toleration,\n        corpMajority: corpMajority,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckExecutiveControl() {\n    if (ppIsExecutiveGraceActive()) {\n        return {\n            chamberId: 'executive',\n            status: 'player_grace',\n            graceActive: true,\n            coalitions: [],\n            affordableCoalitions: [],\n        }\n    }\n\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('executive', null, {})\n    var affordable = coalitions.filter(function(c) { return c.affordable })\n    return {\n        chamberId: 'executive',\n        status: affordable.length ? 'player' : 'corp_flunky',\n        graceActive: false,\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        affordableCoalitions: affordable,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId) {\n    var opts = {\n        reformVote: true,\n        availableSeats: ppReformVoteAvailableSeats(districtId),\n    }\n    return ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, opts)\n}\n\nfunction ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths(districtId) {\n    var coalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var pairs = []\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        for (var j = i + 1; j < coalitions.length; j++) {\n            var a = coalitions[i]\n            var b = coalitions[j]\n            if (!ppPartiesDisjoint(a.partyUse, b.partyUse)) { continue }\n            if (!a.affordable || !b.affordable) { continue }\n            pairs.push({ pathA: a, pathB: b })\n        }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        homeIgs: ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId),\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        validPairs: pairs,\n        possible: pairs.length > 0,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg) {\n    var delta = reprisalByIg || {}\n    return { reprisalDelta: delta }\n}\n\nfunction ppCanMaintainControl(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (coalitions[i].affordable) { return { ok: true, coalition: coalitions[i] }\n        }\n    }\n    return { ok: false, coalition: null }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckPostReformMaintenance(districtId, reprisalByIg, voteCoalitionId) {\n    var opts = ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg)\n    var maintain = ppCanMaintainControl('district', districtId, opts)\n    var voteCoalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var voteCoalition = null\n    for (var i = 0; i < voteCoalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (voteCoalitions[i].id === voteCoalitionId) {\n            voteCoalition = voteCoalitions[i]\n            break\n        }\n    }\n\n    var voteIgs = {}\n    if (voteCoalition) {\n        for (var j = 0; j < voteCoalition.payees.length; j++) {\n            voteIgs[voteCoalition.payees[j].ig] = 1\n        }\n    }\n\n    var maintenanceIgs = {}\n    if (maintain.ok && maintain.coalition) {\n        for (var k = 0; k < maintain.coalition.payees.length; k++) {\n            maintenanceIgs[maintain.coalition.payees[k].ig] = maintain.coalition.payees[k]\n        }\n    }\n\n    var corpPostReformSupport = false\n    var syndicatePostReformSupport = false\n    var igKeys = Object.keys(maintenanceIgs)\n    for (var m = 0; m < igKeys.length; m++) {\n        var ig = igKeys[m]\n        if (voteIgs[ig]) { continue }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate') { corpPostReformSupport = true }\n        if (PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'syndicate') { syndicatePostReformSupport = true }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        maintained: maintain.ok,\n        maintenanceCoalition: maintain.coalition,\n        voteCoalitionId: voteCoalitionId,\n        achievements: {\n            gotEmByTheShortHairs: corpPostReformSupport,\n            anOfferTheyCantRefuse: syndicatePostReformSupport,\n        },\n    }\n}\n\nQ.ppFreeBandwidth = ppFreeBandwidth\nQ.ppBandwidthCostForSeats = ppBandwidthCostForSeats\nQ.ppHomeIgsForDistrict = ppHomeIgsForDistrict\nQ.ppReadDistrictSeats = ppReadDistrictSeats\nQ.ppReadExecutiveSeats = ppReadExecutiveSeats\nQ.ppIsExecutiveGraceActive = ppIsExecutiveGraceActive\nQ.ppTolerationOfferEligible = ppTolerationOfferEligible\nQ.ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions\nQ.ppCheckDistrictControl = ppCheckDistrictControl\nQ.ppCheckExecutiveControl = ppCheckExecutiveControl\nQ.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths = ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths\nQ.ppCanMaintainControl = ppCanMaintainControl\nQ.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance = ppCheckPostReformMaintenance\n\n// MIKASA_POLITICAL_POWER_RUNTIME_END\n\npolitical_power_runtime_ready = 1\n\n!}\ngo-to: jumpScene\n"
}

hasHomeSynd = true }\n    }\n    if (hasHomeCorp) { seats.C = 0 }\n    if (hasHomeSynd) { seats.G = 0 }\n    seats.H = 0\n    return seats\n}\n\n/** Attribute party seats to IGs for bandwidth billing (v1 heuristic). */\nfunction ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs(party, seatsUsed, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var homeIgs = ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId)\n    var igList = PP_PARTY_IGS[party] || []\n    var out = {}\n    if (seatsUsed <= 0 || !igList.length) { return out }\n\n    if (party === 'C' && homeIgs.length && opts.reformVote) {\n        return out\n    }\n\n    if (party === 'G' && homeIgs.length && opts.reformVote) {\n        return out\n    }\n\n    if (party === 'C' && homeIgs.length && opts.tolerationProp) {\n        var homeCorpsOnly = homeIgs.filter(function(ig) { return PP_IG_KIND[ig] === 'corporate' })\n        if (homeCorpsOnly.length) {\n            var homeShare = Math.min(seatsUsed, ppNum(opts.homeCorpSeatsHeld, seatsUsed))\n            if (homeShare > 0) {\n                out[homeCorpsOnly[0]] = (out[homeCorpsOnly[0]] || 0) + homeShare\n            }\n            seatsUsed -= homeShare\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (seatsUsed <= 0) { return out }\n\n    var ranked = igList.slice()\n    if (districtId && homeIgs.length) {\n        ranked.sort(function(a, b) {\n            var ah = homeIgs.indexOf(a) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            var bh = homeIgs.indexOf(b) >= 0 ? 1 : 0\n            return bh - ah\n        })\n    }\n\n    var primary = ranked[0]\n    out[primary] = (out[primary] || 0) + seatsUsed\n    return out\n}\n\nfunction ppMergePayees(target, add) {\n    var keys = Object.keys(add)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        target[ig] = (target[ig] || 0) + add[ig]\n    }\n    return target\n}\n\nfunction ppPayeesToList(payeesByIg) {\n    var list = []\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var seats = payeesByIg[ig]\n        if (seats > 0) {\n            list.push({ ig: ig, seats: seats, cost: ppBandwidthCostForSeats(seats) })\n        }\n    }\n    return list\n}\n\nfunction ppCoalitionAffordable(payeesByIg, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var keys = Object.keys(payeesByIg)\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var ig = keys[i]\n        var cost = ppBandwidthCostForSeats(payeesByIg[ig])\n        if (ppFreeBandwidth(ig, opts) < cost) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\nfunction ppNonDPartiesUsed(partyUse) {\n    var used = []\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUse[k], 0) > 0) { used.push(k) }\n    }\n    return used.sort().join('+')\n}\n\nfunction ppPartiesDisjoint(partyUseA, partyUseB) {\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (ppNum(partyUseA[k], 0) > 0 && ppNum(partyUseB[k], 0) > 0) { return false }\n    }\n    return true\n}\n\n/**\n * Build one player coalition that uses D plus optional C/G/I blocs to reach majority.\n * partyUse: { C?, G?, I? } seat counts consumed from each bloc.\n */\nfunction ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, partyUse, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var used = { D: dSeats, C: 0, G: 0, I: 0, H: 0 }\n    var payees = {}\n    var keys = ['C', 'G', 'I']\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        var want = ppNum(partyUse[k], 0)\n        var have = available[k]\n        if (want <= 0) { continue }\n        if (want > have) { return null }\n        used[k] = want\n        ppMergePayees(payees, ppAttributePartySeatsToIgs(k, want, districtId, opts))\n    }\n\n    var total = used.D + used.C + used.G + used.I\n    if (total < majority) { return null }\n\n    return {\n        id: 'D+' + ppNonDPartiesUsed(used),\n        chamberId: chamberId,\n        districtId: districtId || null,\n        partyUse: { D: used.D, C: used.C, G: used.G, I: used.I },\n        seatTotal: total,\n        payees: ppPayeesToList(payees),\n        affordable: ppCoalitionAffordable(payees, opts),\n    }\n}\n\n/** Minimal seat consumption from each bloc to reach majority given fixed D seats. */\nfunction ppMinimalPartyUse(dSeats, majority, available, pattern) {\n    var need = Math.max(0, majority - dSeats)\n    var use = { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }\n    if (need <= 0) { return use }\n\n    var keys = pattern || ['C', 'G', 'I']\n    for (var i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n        var k = keys[i]\n        if (need <= 0) { break }\n        var take = Math.min(need, ppNum(available[k], 0))\n        use[k] = take\n        need -= take\n    }\n    if (need > 0) { return null }\n    return use\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var majority = ppMajorityForChamber(chamberId)\n    var available = ppSeatsForChamber(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    var dSeats = available.D\n    var coalitions = []\n    var seen = {}\n\n    if (dSeats >= majority) {\n        var outright = ppBuildCoalition(chamberId, districtId, { C: 0, G: 0, I: 0 }, opts)\n        if (outright) { coalitions.push(outright) }\n    }\n\n    var patterns = [\n        ['C'], ['G'], ['I'],\n        ['C', corpSeats: seats.C,\n        demSeats: seats.D,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    if (seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY) 'insufficient_seats' }\n    }\n    return {\n        eligible: true,\n        homeCorp: prop.homeCorp,\n        corpSeats: prop.corpSeats,\n        demSeats: prop.demSeats,\n        governingSeats: prop.seatTotal,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppDistrictFailureFallback(districtId) {\n    if (districtId === PP_CORP_FLUNKY_DISTRICT) { return 'corp_flunky' }\n    return 'anarchy'\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckDistrictControl(districtId) {\n    var seats = ppReadDistrictSeats(districtId)\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, {})\n    var affordable = coalitions.filter(function(c) { return c.affordable })\n    var toleration = ppTolerationOfferEligible(districtId)\n    var corpMajority = seats.C >= PP_DISTRICT_MAJORITY\n\n    var status = ppDistrictFailureFallback(districtId)\n    if (affordable.length) {\n        status = 'player'\n    } else if (corpMajority) {\n        status = 'corp'\n    } else if (toleration.eligible) {\n 'player' : 'corp_flunky',\n        graceActive: false,\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        affordableCoalitions: affordable,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId) {\n    var opts = {\n        reformVote: true,\n        availableSeats: ppReformVoteAvailableSeats(districtId),\n    }\n    return ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', districtId, opts)\n}\n\nfunction ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths(districtId) {\n    var coalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var pairs = []\n\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        for (var j = i + 1; j < coalitions.length; j++) {\n            var a = coalitions[i]\n            var b = coalitions[j]\n            if (!ppPartiesDisjoint(a.partyUse, b.partyUse)) { continue }\n            if (!a.affordable || !b.affordable) { continue }\n            pairs.push({ pathA: a, pathB: b })\n        }\n    }\n\n    return {\n        districtId: districtId,\n        homeIgs: ppHomeIgsForDistrict(districtId),\n        coalitions: coalitions,\n        validPairs: pairs,\n        possible: pairs.length > 0,\n    }\n}\n\nfunction ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg) {\n    var delta = reprisalByIg || {}\n    return { reprisalDelta: delta }\n}\n\nfunction ppCanMaintainControl(chamberId, districtId, opts) {\n    opts = opts || {}\n    var coalitions = ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts)\n    for (var i = 0; i < coalitions.length; i++) {\n        if (coalitions[i].affordable) { return { ok: true, coalition: coalitions[i] }\n        }\n    }\n    return { ok: false, coalition: null }\n}\n\nfunction ppCheckPostReformMaintenance(districtId, reprisalByIg, voteCoalitionId) {\n    var opts = ppApplyReprisalDelta(reprisalByIg)\n    var maintain = ppCanMaintainControl('district', districtId, opts)\n    var voteCoalitions = ppEnumerateReformVoteCoalitions(districtId)\n    var voteCoalition = null\n    for (var i = 0; ## [2026-06-09T01:41:45Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

`ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions(chamberId, districtId, opts)` reads seat counts from `Q`, tries every way to reach a majority using **all D seats** plus the smallest slice of C/G/I needed, and returns an array of coalition objects with payees and an `affordable` flag.

**District majority = 8** (15-seat councils). **Patterns tried:** D alone (if D≥8), then D+C, D+G, D+I, D+C+G, D+C+I, D+G+I, D+C+G+I — each with minimal non-D seats consumed.

---

### Example: hung **Vats** council

Seat line after an election:

| D | C | G | H | I |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 6 | 2 | 1 | 2 |

IG state:

```javascript
Q.Vats_D = 4;  Q.Vats_C = 6;  Q.Vats_G = 2;  Q.Vats_H = 1;  Q.Vats_I = 2
Q.soylent_leverage = 4;  Q.soylent_reprisal = 1   // 3 free bandwidth
Q.families_leverage = 2;  Q.families_reprisal = 2   // 0 free bandwidth
```

Call:

```javascript
Q.ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', 'Vats', {})
```

**Output (two coalitions):**

**1. `D+C` — buy Soylent support**
- Uses all 4 D + **4 of 6** C (minimum to hit 8)
- `payees: [{ ig: 'soylent', seats: 4, cost: 1 }]`
- `affordable: true` — Soylent has 3 bandwidth, needs 1

**2. `D+G+I` — gang + independent path**
- Uses 4 D + 2 G + 2 I = 8
- `payees: [{ ig: 'families', seats: 2, cost: 1 }]`
- `affordable: false` — Families has 0 bandwidth (2−2)

So the district scene could show two control options; the gang path is **seat-feasible but greyed out**.

---

### Same seats, Soylent tapped out

```javascript
Q.soylent_bandwidth_committed = 1   // 1 point already committed elsewhere
Q.soylent_leverage = 3; Q.soylent_reprisal = 2   // 0 free
Q.families_leverage = 5; Q.families_reprisal = 1  // 4 free
```

Now:
- **`D+C`** → `affordable: false` (Soylent broke)
- **`D+G+I`** → `affordable: true` (Families can pay)

Same seat math, flipped UI — that's the bandwidth tradeoff in action.

---

### Example: **Aurora** outright majority

```javascript
Q.Aurora_D = 9;  // … rest irrelevant for control
Q.ppEnumeratePlayerCoalitions('district', 'Aurora', {})
```

Returns one coalition:

- **`D+`** — 9 seats, no payees, `affordable: true`
- No IG bandwidth spent; matches “hold Aurora, don't lose it”

---

### Shape of each returned object

```javascript
{
  id: 'D+C',              // or 'D+G+I', 'D+', etc.
  chamberId: 'district',
  districtId: 'Vats',
  partyUse: { D: 4, C: 4, G: 0, I: 0 },
  seatTotal: 8,
  payees: [{ ig: 'soylent', seats: 4, cost: 1 }],
  affordable: true
}
```

`payees` is who you're **buying** with bandwidth. **I seats cost nothing in v1** (no IG payee). **H is never used** — not in patterns.

---

### Typical call chain in-game (later, when wired)

```javascript
var report = Q.ppCheckDistrictControl('Vats')
// report.coalitions        → everything seat-feasible
// report.affordableCoalitions → subset you can actually afford right now
```

I ran this against the actual runtime block in `political_power_runtime.scene.dry` with those numbers — the output matches what's above. One small implementation quirk: when D already has a majority, outright control and the empty pattern both produce id `D+`; deduping could be tightened later, but it doesn't affect the hung-council case.

## [2026-06-09T01:45:13Z] tool (postToolUse)

**Tool:** `Write`

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/planning/political_power.md",
  "content": "Making the elections actually matter while keeping to the theme of corporate oligarchy will take a little bit of finesse.  The important part will be securing leverage even without a majority coalition.  There are multiple dynamics in play:\n\n1. The Aurora district is an outlier where the democrats control it from the start of the game, the player just needs to keep from losing it.  Aurora should be **partly reformed** at new game — past democratic wins already in place (judiciary, modest worker protections, etc.).  The player starts with control and can push further reforms, but those still add reprisal; probably **less reprisal per reform than elsewhere**, since incremental follow-ups are less sweeping than breaking corp fief rule cold.\n2. The Deeps is a special case where it's about winning the trust of the Deepers (something to tackle later)\n3. The Pitts and the Docks are a bit strange so the player might have lots of support there or none at all.  But even there the player will mostly want to leverage point 4.  The Docks differs again in failure mode: if nobody can govern, a corporate flunky retakes the district (same class of fallback as city executive), not anarchy.\n4. The real important dynamic is in the corporate districts and the citywide government.  Here it's all about securing leverage by making sure nobody else has a clean majority, letting you trade away toleration agreements for leverage.\n\n\nIn the corporate home districts the corporations have a huge advantage because of the drones vote.  But if the drones start abstaining en masse and no longer constitute a majority of voters the vote shifts to the 1/2/3rd waves and coops, much less corporation friendly demographics.  \n\nIf a coorporation is knocked out of a majority in it's home district and the player has enough seats to give them a majority, they will be given the option to offer toleration to current district leadership.  This results in a big chunk of leverage on the corporation which lasts as long as the player is keeping them in power.  Losing control of their home district government should be a big blow to the corps.  \n\nWith the city government, the player gets a free year of control after the tether snap event but after that they can continue to control the government if they have enough leverage on corps/syndicates to buy a majority.  If they dont, a corporate flunky is put back in charge.  The player could also negotiate toleration like with district governments.\n\nTo control a district government (without winning an outright majority), the player will need to have leverage to cash in with corporations and syndicates.  The player needs to maintain a leverage over reprisal surplus of a certain size to afford support for control of a district or the city.  If the player can no longer afford the support they will lose the government (unless they can negotiate a new agreement.)  The leverage needs to be paid for every group whose support you needs and scales by how big a share of the seats they have.\n\nWith control of the government of a district or the city, the player can start implementing pro-worker, pro-democratic and anti-crime reforms.  Passing a reform will usually raise the reprisal of syndicates and/or corporations so you need to have excess support sufficient to maintain your government after the reprisal from the reform.  If you can't maintain the government afterwards you can't get the reform implemented.\n\nPro-worker reforms will shift money from the corporation to the voters living in the corporation's home district and the docks.  This will weaken the corporation as well as give reprisal.  The other corps will also gain reprisal unless they are at war with the target.\n\nAnti-crime reforms likewise weaken the syndicates and give reprisal but unlike the corporations the syndicates mostly dont have a stranglehold on a district government.  If you have corporate toleration without dependence on the gangs the corporations wont gain reprisal for you doing anti-crime stuff.\n\nPro-democratic reforms are only possible if the player has enough support to secure a majority both of these ways:\n1) Support from gangs (and independents) without any corporation (or hate) support needed\n2) Support from corporations who dont have the district as their home district (plus independents) without any gang (or hate) support needed.\nSupport for both of these needs to be high enough before the vote.  Additionally you need support high enough to maintain control of the government after the vote (possibly by making a big concession like giving up a government control elsewhere).  Pro democratic reforms will change the electorate (like freeing the drones to vote as they please) and will cause a big reprisal jump for all the gangs and corps.  However if you can maintain control of the government until the next election, suddenly you might find yourself much less depedent on negotiating for support.\n\nBesides these big reforms, just bare control of the government would allow for some smaller stuff like improving the judiciary which wont cause reprisal spikes.  Additionally control of the government would make efforts to improve the police/militia become more effective.\n\nIf the player can't control the government and no corporation has enough votes to control a district government even with toleration from the player, there will instead be an anarchy situation where the corporations and gangs start bribing the district politicians left right and center.  This will be very bad for a home district corporation and very good for the syndicates.  If enough governments collapse this way the gangs might become the main threat not the corporations.  **Exception: the Docks** — like citywide government, the fallback is a **corporate flunky from before** (pre-tether default leadership), not anarchy. District anarchy applies only to the other districts.\n\nThe first step to this is implement a function that checks what, if any coalitions are possible for the player in that district.  This gets checked every time the district votes, allowing the player to negotiate for control if they have the leverage.  If a coalition is possible but the leverage isn't there, it will appear greyed out.  The player can use the district scene to negotiate for a new coalition at any time, including if they weren't previously controlling the government.\n\nsecuring control with leverage would not lower the leverage but it would use up your \"bandwidth\" of leverage above reprisal.  So if their attitude is transactional and you use 3 leverage to secure support their attitude stays transactional, you just can't use up that leverage elsewhere, you keep using it as long as you are using their support to control the government.  If you voluntarily give up the government or can no longer afford it, that \"bandwidth\" becomes available to use elsewhere.\n\nCalendar: district control will always be updated after a district election or after the player can't afford the government.  The player can also use the district menu to update this sooner.\n\nThe free year after tether snap is for control of the city government.  The corporations need to leave you in government at least that long to placate the non-Mikasa corporations.  Even if reprisal exceeds leverage they will leave you in place for the one year grace period.  After that yearlong grace period you'll need to negotiate for control again.  You are forced to renegotiate for control if you no longer have enough leverage \"bandwidth\" to control the government.  The player could also voluntarily negotiate for control (not sure how this will be triggered).\n\nThe leverage loop is heavily influenced by the fact that trading away toleration is the easiest way to get leverage.  The major phases will result in some political shifts but mostly in order to have control somewhere you need to tolerate somewhere else.  Increasing vote share increases how much leverage you can get by trading away toleration and reduces how much leverage you need to secure support for your own governments.\n\nThe Hate groups wont ever support you because they are modeled on the Nazi strategy of refusing to accept a minority role in someone elses coalition while planning the violent siezure of power.  So keeping their vote share down is important because any seats they hold are unavailable for making deals.\n\nI'm not covering any possibilities besides toleration, control and anarchy because I'm afraid of scope creep.  I'm going to assume that generally the corps and gangs are at each other's throats so much that the player can always negotiate a toleration agreement with them.  If the corporations or gangs are more afraid of the player then of each other then you will be rapidly arriving at the endgame where they go to war with you.\n\nCovering the loss of control in Aurora is something I think can be left for later.\n\nbandwidth is per group.  If you need the support from the 7 families, you can't use leverage with the Sharks to get that support.\n\nThe reforms shrink the bandwidth because they raise reprisal.  Bandwidth is just the term I was using for the gap between leverage and reprisal.\n\nAt the grace year expiration, the player would need to immediately negotiate for control of the government or the corporations will agree on putting a \"neutral\" corporate flunky in charge, this is the same figure who was in charge before the tether snap incident.\n\nVoluntarily negotiating for control is about getting leverage by going to toleration or swapping support because you need to free up leverage with a group.\n\nIf Aurora toleration is implemented it should be something of a move of desperation, a corporation or syndicate in control of Aurora will do tons of damage at a very fast pace.\n\n## Pro-democratic dual path (general rule)\n\nBefore a pro-democratic reform vote, two distinct majority coalitions must exist:\n\n- Both reach majority on seat math; the player only **uses one** to pass the bill.\n- Non-D members must be **disjoint** between the two paths (D may appear in both).\n- **H** is excluded from both paths.\n- Any **home-district IG** (corp or syndicate) is excluded from both paths — they will not vote for pro-democratic reforms in their home territory. If multiple IGs call a district home (e.g. Pitts), **none** of them vote for reforms there.\n- Pre-vote: both paths must be affordable (bandwidth to buy non-D support). Post-vote: separate maintenance check after the reprisal spike — the preview paths need not still work afterward.\n- Edge case: a home corp/syndicate may still **prop up the government** after the reform if leverage is high enough (they protect their investment), even though they never voted for the reform. Vote coalition ≠ maintenance coalition.\n\n## Attitude vs affordability\n\n`wary` or `contempt` attitude does **not** block buying support — it usually means `leverage − reprisal` is too small to afford the bandwidth cost. Grey out options on bandwidth, not attitude label.\n\n## Toleration headcount\n\nWhen offering toleration to a home corp that lost its outright majority, **Dem seats count** toward the bloc keeping that leadership in power (e.g. 6C + 4D reaches 10).\n\n## Achievements (add later)\n\n| Title | Icon | Unlock |\n|-------|------|--------|\n| Got 'em by the short hairs. | clothespin | After a pro-democratic reform, a **corporation** supports your government in the post-reform maintenance check without having been in the reform vote coalition. |\n| An offer they can't refuse. | cottonball | Same, but a **syndicate**. |\n\n## Implementation\n\nDraft control-check API (not wired into boot/tick yet): `source/scenes/political_power_runtime.scene.dry` — `Q.ppCheckDistrictControl`, `Q.ppCheckExecutiveControl`, `Q.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths`, `Q.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance`, etc."
}
```

## [2026-06-09T02:11:47Z] user (beforeSubmitPrompt)

okay, make a note somewhere for an anti-crime reform idea:
In Aurora you can pass a reform to establish a witness protection program for a small reprisal cost with the gangs.  You can then expand that witness protection program two ways:
1) pass another reform in Aurora which generates more reprisal
2) Negotiate with the other cycler waystations to set up a joint witness protection program (a use for "geopolitical" options in the media and other places TBD), something that is done secretly and creates no reprisal.

If the witness protection program is established and expanded, other districts will gain the option to join the witness protection program.  This would be a bargain in terms of reprisal cost for it's anti-crime impact.

Also this will be used to determine if the station manager gets protected if you break the turn a blind eye agreement.

This will weaken the corporation as well as give reprisal.  The other corps will also gain reprisal unless they are at war with the target.\n\nAnti-crime reforms likewise weaken the syndicates and give reprisal but unlike the corporations the syndicates mostly dont have a stranglehold on a district government.  If you have corporate toleration without dependence on the gangs the corporations wont gain reprisal for you doing anti-crime stuff.\n\nPro-democratic reforms are only possible if the player has enough support to secure a majority both of these ways:\n1) Support from gangs (and independents) without any corporation (or hate) support needed\n2) Support from corporations who dont have the district as their home district (plus independents) without any gang (or hate) support needed.\nSupport for both of these needs to be high enough before the vote.  Additionally you need support high enough to maintain control of the government after the vote (possibly by making a big concession like giving up a government control elsewhere).  Pro democratic reforms will change the electorate (like freeing the drones to vote as they please) and will cause a big reprisal jump for all the gangs and corps.  However if you can maintain control of the government until the next election, suddenly you might find yourself much less depedent on negotiating for support.\n\nBesides these big reforms, just bare control of the government would allow for some smaller stuff like improving the judiciary which wont cause reprisal spikes.  Additionally control of the government would make efforts to improve the police/militia become more effective.\n\nIf the player can't control the government and no corporation has enough votes to control a district government even with toleration from the player, there will instead be an anarchy situation where the corporations and gangs start bribing the district politicians left right and center.  This will be very bad for a home district corporation and very good for the syndicates.  If enough governments collapse this way the gangs might become the main threat not the corporations.  **Exception: the Docks** — like citywide government, the fallback is a **corporate flunky from before** (pre-tether default leadership), not anarchy. District anarchy applies only to the other districts.\n\nThe first step to this is implement a function that checks what, if any coalitions are possible for the player in that district.  This gets checked every time the district votes, allowing the player to negotiate for control if they have the leverage.  If a coalition is possible but the leverage isn't there, it will appear greyed out.  The player can use the district scene to negotiate for a new coalition at any time, including if they weren't previously controlling the government.\n\nsecuring control with leverage would not lower the leverage but it would use up your \"bandwidth\" of leverage above reprisal.  So if their attitude is transactional and you use 3 leverage to secure support their attitude stays transactional, you just can't use up that leverage elsewhere, you keep using it as long as you are using their support to control the government.  If you voluntarily give up the government or can no longer afford it, that \"bandwidth\" becomes available to use elsewhere.\n\nCalendar: district control will always be updated after a district election or after the player can't afford the government.  The player can also use the district menu to update this sooner.\n\nThe free year after tether snap is for control of the city government.  The corporations need to leave you in government at least that long to placate the non-Mikasa corporations.  Even if reprisal exceeds leverage they will leave you in place for the one year grace period.  After that yearlong grace period you'll need to negotiate for control again.  You are forced to renegotiate for control if you no longer have enough leverage \"bandwidth\" to control the government.  The player could also voluntarily negotiate for control (not sure how this will be triggered).\n\nThe leverage loop is heavily influenced by the fact that trading away toleration is the easiest way to get leverage.  The major phases will result in some political shifts but mostly in order to have control somewhere you need to tolerate somewhere else.  Increasing vote share increases how much leverage you can get by trading away toleration and reduces how much leverage you need to secure support for your own governments.\n\nThe Hate groups wont ever support you because they are modeled on the Nazi strategy of refusing to accept a minority role in someone elses coalition while planning the violent siezure of power.  So keeping their vote share down is important because any seats they hold are unavailable for making deals.\n\nI'm not covering any possibilities besides toleration, control and anarchy because I'm afraid of scope creep.  I'm going to assume that generally the corps and gangs are at each other's throats so much that the player can always negotiate a toleration agreement with them.  If the corporations or gangs are more afraid of the player then of each other then you will be rapidly arriving at the endgame where they go to war with you.\n\nCovering the loss of control in Aurora is something I think can be left for later.\n\nbandwidth is per group.  If you need the support from the 7 families, you can't use leverage with the Sharks to get that support.\n\nThe reforms shrink the bandwidth because they raise reprisal.  Bandwidth is just the term I was using for the gap between leverage and reprisal.\n\nAt the grace year expiration, the player would need to immediately negotiate for control of the government or the corporations will agree on putting a \"neutral\" corporate flunky in charge, this is the same figure who was in charge before the tether snap incident.\n\nVoluntarily negotiating for control is about getting leverage by going to toleration or swapping support because you need to free up leverage with a group.\n\nIf Aurora toleration is implemented it should be something of a move of desperation, a corporation or syndicate in control of Aurora will do tons of damage at a very fast pace.\n\n## Aurora at game start\n\nTreat Aurora as **partly reformed**: the democrats already hold the council and some reforms are baked in.  The player does not need to negotiate control there initially, but can enact additional reforms from day one.  Each new reform still spikes reprisal (and needs a post-reform bandwidth check if we apply that rule uniformly), though reprisal magnitudes should be **lower than corporate-district equivalents** — tuning TBD per reform card.  Losing Aurora later remains out of scope for v1; holding it while reprisal accumulates is the early-game pressure.\n\n## Witness protection (anti-crime reform, TBD)\n\nAurora-first anti-crime reform chain — good fit for partly-reformed district, modest early reprisal, high payoff if expanded.\n\n**Establish (Aurora council reform)** — Pass a reform to set up a **witness protection program**. Small reprisal cost with the **gangs** (syndicate reprisal, not corps if coalition has no gang dependence per usual anti-crime rules). Requires Aurora control (outright D majority at start).\n\n**Expand (two paths):**\n\n1. **Local reform** — Another Aurora council reform; scales the program; **more gang reprisal**.\n2. **Geopolitical** — Negotiate with other **cycler waystations** for a joint witness-protection network. Surfaced via **geopolitical** options in peer media and elsewhere (TBD). Done **secretly** → **no reprisal** (off-books cooperation between stations).\n\n**District adoption** — Once the program is **established and expanded** (both tiers complete? local + geopolitical OR two local steps? — tune), other districts gain the option to **join** the witness protection program. Joining should be a **bargain**: low reprisal cost relative to anti-crime impact (crime_paramilitary / `crime_relief_*` hooks TBD).\n\n**Blind-eye payoff** — Program state determines whether the **station manager survives** if the player **breaks** the turn-a-blind-eye promise ([`cable_syndicate_blind_eye.scene.dry`](source/scenes/events/cable_syndicate_blind_eye.scene.dry)). Today that check is `police_reform >= 2` and `police_investigated >= 1` (Marcus Hale); **replace or gate on witness-protection flags** when this reform ships (e.g. `witness_protection_established` + expanded, or district joined). Breaking the promise stays high-stakes; witness protection is the intended humane out.\n\nSuggested `Q` flags (names TBD): `witness_protection_aurora`, `witness_protection_expanded`, `witness_protection_geopolitical`, per-district `witness_protection_join_*`.\n\n## Pro-democratic dual path (general rule)\n\nBefore a pro-democratic reform vote, two distinct majority coalitions must exist:\n\n- Both reach majority on seat math; the player only **uses one** to pass the bill.\n- Non-D members must be **disjoint** between the two paths (D may appear in both).\n- **H** is excluded from both paths.\n- Any **home-district IG** (corp or syndicate) is excluded from both paths — they will not vote for pro-democratic reforms in their home territory. If multiple IGs call a district home (e.g. Pitts), **none** of them vote for reforms there.\n- Pre-vote: both paths must be affordable (bandwidth to buy non-D support). Post-vote: separate maintenance check after the reprisal spike — the preview paths need not still work afterward.\n- Edge case: a home corp/syndicate may still **prop up the government** after the reform if leverage is high enough (they protect their investment), even though they never voted for the reform. Vote coalition ≠ maintenance coalition.\n\n## Attitude vs affordability\n\n`wary` or `contempt` attitude does **not** block buying support — it usually means `leverage − reprisal` is too small to afford the bandwidth cost. Grey out options on bandwidth, not attitude label.\n\n## Toleration headcount\n\nWhen offering toleration to a home corp that lost its outright majority, **Dem seats count** toward the bloc keeping that leadership in power (e.g. 6C + 4D reaches 10).\n\n## Achievements (add later)\n\n| Title | Icon | Unlock |\n|-------|------|--------|\n| Got 'em by the short hairs. | clothespin | After a pro-democratic reform, a **corporation** supports your government in the post-reform maintenance check without having been in the reform vote coalition. |\n| An offer they can't refuse. | cottonball | Same, but a **syndicate**. |\n\n## Implementation\n\nDraft control-check API (not wired into boot/tick yet): `source/scenes/political_power_runtime.scene.dry` — `Q.ppCheckDistrictControl`, `Q.ppCheckExecutiveControl`, `Q.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths`, `Q.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance`, etc."
}
```

Pro democratic reforms will change the electorate (like freeing the drones to vote as they please) and will cause a big reprisal jump for all the gangs and corps.  However if you can maintain control of the government until the next election, suddenly you might find yourself much less depedent on negotiating for support.\n\nBesides these big reforms, just bare control of the government would allow for some smaller stuff like improving the judiciary which wont cause reprisal spikes.  Additionally control of the government would make efforts to improve the police/militia become more effective.\n\nIf the player can't control the government and no corporation has enough votes to control a district government even with toleration from the player, there will instead be an anarchy situation where the corporations and gangs start bribing the district politicians left right and center.  This will be very bad for a home district corporation and very good for the syndicates.  If enough governments collapse this way the gangs might become the main threat not the corporations.  **Exception: the Docks** — like citywide government, the fallback is a **corporate flunky from before** (pre-tether default leadership), not anarchy. District anarchy applies only to the other districts.\n\nThe first step to this is implement a function that checks what, if any coalitions are possible for the player in that district.  This gets checked every time the district votes, allowing the player to negotiate for control if they have the leverage.  If a coalition is possible but the leverage isn't there, it will appear greyed out.  The player can use the district scene to negotiate for a new coalition at any time, including if they weren't previously controlling the government.\n\nsecuring control with leverage would not lower the leverage but it would use up your \"bandwidth\" of leverage above reprisal.  So if their attitude is transactional and you use 3 leverage to secure support their attitude stays transactional, you just can't use up that leverage elsewhere, you keep using it as long as you are using their support to control the government.  If you voluntarily give up the government or can no longer afford it, that \"bandwidth\" becomes available to use elsewhere.\n\nCalendar: district control will always be updated after a district election or after the player can't afford the government.  The player can also use the district menu to update this sooner.\n\nThe free year after tether snap is for control of the city government.  The corporations need to leave you in government at least that long to placate the non-Mikasa corporations.  Even if reprisal exceeds leverage they will leave you in place for the one year grace period.  After that yearlong grace period you'll need to negotiate for control again.  You are forced to renegotiate for control if you no longer have enough leverage \"bandwidth\" to control the government.  The player could also voluntarily negotiate for control (not sure how this will be triggered).\n\nThe leverage loop is heavily influenced by the fact that trading away toleration is the easiest way to get leverage.  The major phases will result in some political shifts but mostly in order to have control somewhere you need to tolerate somewhere else.  Increasing vote share increases how much leverage you can get by trading away toleration and reduces how much leverage you need to secure support for your own governments.\n\nThe Hate groups wont ever support you because they are modeled on the Nazi strategy of refusing to accept a minority role in someone elses coalition while planning the violent siezure of power.  So keeping their vote share down is important because any seats they hold are unavailable for making deals.\n\nI'm not covering any possibilities besides toleration, control and anarchy because I'm afraid of scope creep.  I'm going to assume that generally the corps and gangs are at each other's throats so much that the player can always negotiate a toleration agreement with them.  If the corporations or gangs are more afraid of the player then of each other then you will be rapidly arriving at the endgame where they go to war with you.\n\nCovering the loss of control in Aurora is something I think can be left for later.\n\nbandwidth is per group.  If you need the support from the 7 families, you can't use leverage with the Sharks to get that support.\n\nThe reforms shrink the bandwidth because they raise reprisal.  Bandwidth is just the term I was using for the gap between leverage and reprisal.\n\nAt the grace year expiration, the player would need to immediately negotiate for control of the government or the corporations will agree on putting a \"neutral\" corporate flunky in charge, this is the same figure who was in charge before the tether snap incident.\n\nVoluntarily negotiating for control is about getting leverage by going to toleration or swapping support because you need to free up leverage with a group.\n\nIf Aurora toleration is implemented it should be something of a move of desperation, a corporation or syndicate in control of Aurora will do tons of damage at a very fast pace.\n\n## Aurora at game start\n\nTreat Aurora as **partly reformed**: the democrats already hold the council and some reforms are baked in.  The player does not need to negotiate control there initially, but can enact additional reforms from day one.  Each new reform still spikes reprisal (and needs a post-reform bandwidth check if we apply that rule uniformly), though reprisal magnitudes should be **lower than corporate-district equivalents** — tuning TBD per reform card.  Losing Aurora later remains out of scope for v1; holding it while reprisal accumulates is the early-game pressure.\n\n## Witness protection (anti-crime reform, TBD)\n\nSingle track: **`witness_protection_level`** (0 = none). Each step below bumps the level; higher = larger, more capable program. Aurora-first; good fit for partly-reformed district.\n\n| Level bump | How | Reprisal |\n|------------|-----|----------|\n| 0 → 1 | Aurora council reform — establish pilot program | Small, **gangs** |\n| +1 | Aurora council reform — grow local capacity | More gang reprisal |\n| +1 | Geopolitical deal — joint network with other **cycler waystations** (peer media etc., TBD) | **None** (secret) |\n| per district | Other districts **join** program (unlock when level ≥ 2) | Bargain — low reprisal, strong anti-crime |\n\n**District join** unlocks at **level ≥ 2** (established + at least one growth step). Joining a district is cheap reprisal for solid `crime_relief_*` / paramilitary impact (hooks TBD).\n\n**Blind-eye payoff** — Level gates whether the **station manager survives** if the player breaks the blind-eye promise ([`cable_syndicate_blind_eye.scene.dry`](source/scenes/events/cable_syndicate_blind_eye.scene.dry)); replaces today's `police_reform` / `police_investigated` check for Marcus Hale. Threshold TBD (likely level ≥ 2).\n\nState: `witness_protection_level`; optional per-district `witness_protection_join_*` (or fold joins into level / district flags when implementing).\n\n## Pro-democratic dual path (general rule)\n\nBefore a pro-democratic reform vote, two distinct majority coalitions must exist:\n\n- Both reach majority on seat math; the player only **uses one** to pass the bill.\n- Non-D members must be **disjoint** between the two paths (D may appear in both).\n- **H** is excluded from both paths.\n- Any **home-district IG** (corp or syndicate) is excluded from both paths — they will not vote for pro-democratic reforms in their home territory. If multiple IGs call a district home (e.g. Pitts), **none** of them vote for reforms there.\n- Pre-vote: both paths must be affordable (bandwidth to buy non-D support). Post-vote: separate maintenance check after the reprisal spike — the preview paths need not still work afterward.\n- Edge case: a home corp/syndicate may still **prop up the government** after the reform if leverage is high enough (they protect their investment), even though they never voted for the reform. Vote coalition ≠ maintenance coalition.\n\n## Attitude vs affordability\n\n`wary` or `contempt` attitude does **not** block buying support — it usually means `leverage − reprisal` is too small to afford the bandwidth cost. Grey out options on bandwidth, not attitude label.\n\n## Toleration headcount\n\nWhen offering toleration to a home corp that lost its outright majority, **Dem seats count** toward the bloc keeping that leadership in power (e.g. 6C + 4D reaches 10).\n\n## Achievements (add later)\n\n| Title | Icon | Unlock |\n|-------|------|--------|\n| Got 'em by the short hairs. | clothespin | After a pro-democratic reform, a **corporation** supports your government in the post-reform maintenance check without having been in the reform vote coalition. |\n| An offer they can't refuse. | cottonball | Same, but a **syndicate**. |\n\n## Implementation\n\nDraft control-check API (not wired into boot/tick yet): `source/scenes/political_power_runtime.scene.dry` — `Q.ppCheckDistrictControl`, `Q.ppCheckExecutiveControl`, `Q.ppFindProDemocraticDualPaths`, `Q.ppCheckPostReformMaintenance`, etc."

## [2026-06-09T12:05:51Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Here’s what’s wrong with **Election simulation** from the root menu, in severity order.

---

### 1. **Runtime never loads — main breakage**

From `root.start_menu`, `started` is **0** and nothing runs `setup_functions` → `election_runtime`.

The sim only runs projections in `@2300`:

```6:7:source/scenes/election_simulation.scene.dry
@2300
on-arrival: {! Q.update_projections({ allDistricts: true }) !}
```

`Q.update_projections` is defined only when `election_runtime.scene.dry` has been visited. On a **fresh page load → menu → Election simulation → “2300”**, you get `Q.update_projections is not a function` (verified in Node).

Contrast **`@dev_cable_neg`** in root, which sets `Q.started = 1` and jumps into content that eventually pulls runtimes. Election sim does no bootstrap at all.

---

### 2. **Extra click for no reason**

Entering `election_simulation` shows a single choice, **“2300”**, with no prose. Results live in `@show_results`, which you only reach after clicking it. For a debug tool from the title menu, you’d normally want `on-arrival` on the scene (or auto `go-to: show_results` after bootstrap) so it runs immediately.

---

### 3. **Dead end — no way back to menu**

`@show_results` has **no choices** and no `@backSpecialScene` / link to `root.start_menu_2`.

Compare `library`, `district_map`, `mod_loader` — all use `is-special: true` and a back choice. Election sim has neither, so you’re stuck unless you reload or hack navigation.

---

### 4. **`is-special: true` missing (doc mismatch)**

`source/lib/election-v2.md` calls this a “debug **special** scene”, but the file has no `is-special: true` (unlike `status`, `library`, `district_map`). So it doesn’t follow the same special-scene / back-navigation pattern as the other tools.

---

### 5. **Weaker than Status for actual debugging**

`status.scene.dry` `@polls` calls `Q.update_projections()` and shows **poll percentages**, stratum breakdown, drone abstention, district polls, etc.

Election sim only shows **`Proj_*` seat counts** plus the population table. No `poll_*` / `*_poll_*` display, so it’s a narrow slice of what the engine produces — fine as a seat projector, but easy to misread as “full election debug” when Status is richer.

---

### 6. **Electorate / story stacks ignored from menu**

Even after fixing bootstrap, loading **only** `election_runtime` skips `electorate_runtime`, so `applyElectorateModifiers` never runs and **stack-driven poll shifts** from cards/events won’t appear. That’s OK for “vanilla demographics” if intentional; misleading if you expect sim results to match mid-game saves.

---

### 7. **Smaller UX / copy issues**

| Issue | Detail |
|--------|--------|
| Label “2300” | Doesn’t set `Q.year`; doesn’t explain “run baseline projection”. |
| Typo | “xenopobic” in results prose. |
| Population table | Strata with 0 pop (e.g. Aurora `third_wave`) show blank/`0` — correct but easy to read as broken. |
| Seat vs poll | Results are **projected seats** (`Proj_*`), not percents; no label says that (Status explains its tables). |

---

### Flow diagram

```text
root.start_menu (started=0)
    → election_simulation
        → [click "2300"]
            → Q.update_projections()  ← undefined unless election_runtime ran earlier in session
        → show_results (no exit)
```

---

### Suggested fix shape (when you want to implement)

1. On entering election sim from menu: set `Q.mikasa_restore_scene = 'election_simulation.show_results'` (or similar) and `go-to: setup_functions` with a flag to load **election** (+ optionally **electorate**) only, without starting the full game.
2. Auto-run `update_projections` on arrival after bootstrap.
3. Add `is-special: true` and `- @backSpecialScene: Back to menu` (or `go-to: root.start_menu_2`).
4. Optionally mirror Status poll tables or link to Status.

I can wire that bootstrap path if you want it fixed next.
